| 2026-11-10 | Follow-up experiment 13, why the top-ranked sample flips (label `why`), specified after the main-study and follow-up outcomes and the paper's self-review were read, and before any run; reported as a follow-up, outside the pre-registered study, whatever it shows. Questions: (P) on a platform that recompiles bit-identically, how does the top-1 flip rate grow with a perturbation of known size at the trunk output, and at what size does it reach the observed cross-platform rates; (L) where in the trunk do two platforms start to differ, and how fast does the difference grow, in units of the bfloat16 unit roundoff u = 2^-8; (F) with the trunk in float32 on a TPU and on a GPU, does the platform effect fall, and at what cost in time and memory. Every arm keeps the main study's settings (AlphaFold 3 v3.0.4 with `af3_tpu/af3_tpu.patch`, the same weights, software stacks, frozen inputs of `inputs/manifest.csv`, recycles, samples, machine types and image families, one fresh process per (target, seed, repetition) with an empty compile cache); only the settings named here change. (a) Mechanism, no AlphaFold 3 source edited: a new harness module, imported by `harness/af3_entry.py` only when one of its environment variables is set, so every earlier configuration runs exactly as before; the variables are set by new configurations in `harness/configs.yaml` and recorded in `session.json` (`env_overrides`). (a1) Perturbation: before the diffusion, confidence and distogram heads read the trunk output (the single and pair representations after the last recycle, float32, `model.py` lines 289 to 343), each of the two arrays X is replaced by X + eps * (norm(m * X) / norm(m * Z)) * (m * Z), where m is the token mask (single) or its outer product (pair), norms are Frobenius norms over every entry, and Z is standard normal noise of X's shape, float32, from `jax.random` with the key fold_in(fold_in(PRNGKey(2026), h), s), h the first 4 bytes of the SHA-256 of the input's `name` read as an unsigned integer, s the process's AlphaFold 3 seed, split once into a single key and a pair key. The relative L2 size of the perturbation over the real tokens is therefore eps, exactly up to float32 rounding, for both arrays; Z does not depend on eps (the same draw at every level); the diffusion noise is unchanged (the perturbation key is not drawn from AlphaFold 3's random stream). The trunk input is not perturbed (it is identical across platforms, follow-up 5). (a2) Capture: the single and pair representations at depth d = 0 (the input of the 48-block Pairformer, after the input embedding, the recycling embedding and the 4-block MSA module) and d = 1 to 48 (after Pairformer block d), in recycle 0 (the first pass, where every platform starts from identical inputs) and recycle 10 (the last), and the trunk output after every recycle r = 0 to 10; single at every depth over all real tokens; pair at even depths only, on a fixed token subset S identical on every platform: every ligand token plus 64 protein tokens at positions round(linspace(0, n_protein - 1, 64)) in token order (all protein tokens if there are at most 64); stored as the bfloat16 values the trunk computes (bit-exact), with the target, seed, recycle and depth of each array. Expected size about 0.2 GB per process, at most about 10 GB for the arm. (a3) Float32 trunk: AlphaFold 3's own global setting `GlobalConfig.bfloat16`, default `all`, set to `none` (`intermediate` is rejected by the Evoformer), which runs the input embedding, the trunk (MSA module and Pairformer) and the confidence head's Pairformer on float32 activations; the stored bfloat16 weights are used as they are (exact in float32); together with `JAX_DEFAULT_MATMUL_PRECISION=highest` (section 11), so the float32 matmuls run as six-pass bfloat16 on TPU and IEEE float32 on the L4 instead of one-pass bfloat16 and TensorFloat-32. The diffusion module is float32 already and is unchanged apart from the matmul precision, as in V6h and Gh. (b) Arms. P (perturbation) on V6 (TPU v6e, `tpu_xla` plus the perturbation): the 60 targets, seeds 1 and 2, r1, at eps = 0 (P0) and at six levels, 2^-14, 2^-11, 2^-8, 2^-7, 2^-6 and 2^-5 (1/64 u to 8 u; the trunk-output differences between platforms measured in follow-up 2 are about 1 u for the single and 7 to 9 u for the pair representation, results/analysis/embed_20261015/report.md, so the finer steps sit where the crossing is expected). P0 runs on every target, so no contingent extension is needed. L (layer-wise) with the capture: the 10 pilot targets, seed 1, on V5 (`tpu_xla`, v5e), V6 (`tpu_xla`, v6e) and G (`l4_xla`, L4) in r1, and G in r2. F (float32 trunk): V6f (v6e) and Gf (L4), the 60 targets, seeds 1 to 5 (the main study's seeds; P keeps seeds 1 and 2), r1; Gf also r2 on all 60 targets, seeds 1 to 5; V6f r2 on the 10 pilot targets, seed 1 (TPU recompilation was bit-identical in every earlier arm; if any V6f r2 differs from its r1, V6f r2 is extended to all 60 targets, recorded in a row before that run, with its own budget check). Out-of-memory and other failures are recorded with their cause, never hidden or rerun with other settings; a (target, seed) missing on either side of a contrast leaves it (section 8) and the counts are reported. (c) Checks, before any result is read: every process exits 0 or its failure is recorded; one machine type per condition; P0 bit-identical to the main study's V6 r1 for every (target, seed) (all output files, as the strict check of the pilot); L on V5 and V6 bit-identical to the main study's V5 r1 and V6 r1 for seed 1 (the capture does not change the numerics; G is not run-to-run reproducible and cannot be checked); every capture file present with the expected recycles and depths; V6f r2 against V6f r1 as in (b). A failed P0 check does not stop the analysis: P0 is the reference of P in every case, and the number of P0 processes that differ from the main study is reported. A failed L check is reported and L is then read as a comparison of the instrumented program across platforms. (d) Statistics: as section 5, the target-clustered bootstrap with 10,000 replicates and seed 2026, every contrast paired on (target, seed) with the per-seed top-1 (M2). P: for each level, the top-1 flip rate, the success flip rate and flips that matter of the level against P0, with M4 and M5 medians; the levels are resampled on the same targets in each replicate. L: the relative L2 difference D(A, B) = norm(A - B) / norm(A), A the first-named condition (as follow-up 2), over the captured entries, in units of u, for V5 against V6, V6 against G, G against G r2 (the recompilation level) and, secondary, V5 against G. F: HW(V6f,Gf), RC(Gf), HW(V6,G) and RC(G) on seeds 1 to 5 (V6 and G from the main study, r1 and r2), and D_F = [HW(V6f,Gf) minus RC(Gf)] minus [HW(V6,G) minus RC(G)], all on the same resampled targets. A rough estimate from the main study's interval for HW(V6,G) minus RC(G) (13.3 points, 8.7 to 18.0, five seeds; half-width 4.7, times the square root of 2 for a difference of two such quantities) puts the 95% half-width of D_F for the top-1 flip rate at about 7 percentage points, so a reduction of about half of the 13.3-point effect or more can read as reduced. (e) Reading rules, fixed now. P, descriptive: the curves of top-1 flip rate and success flip rate against eps, with intervals; eps* for each primary pair, the level at which the P curve reaches HW(V5,V6) and HW(V6,G) of the main study on seeds 1 and 2 (the same seeds and targets; the five-seed values 18.0% and 20.7% are reported next to it): the log2-linear interpolation between the first two adjacent levels whose estimates bracket the target rate, searched from the smallest level; reported as below 2^-14 or above 2^-5 when the curve does not bracket it; its interval from the same bootstrap replicates, recomputing the curve and the HW rate on the resampled targets, censored replicates counted at the bound; stated as "the cross-platform rate HW(P,Q) of X% corresponds to a random perturbation of relative size about k times the bfloat16 unit roundoff u at the trunk output (95% interval a to b)", next to the trunk-output differences actually measured between those platforms (L at r = 10, follow-up 2). L, descriptive: per depth, the median and range over the 10 targets of D in units of u, for recycle 0 and recycle 10, single and pair; the trunk output after each recycle; for each target, the first depth in recycle 0 at which D(V5,V6), and D(V6,G), exceeds D(G, G r2) at that depth (where D(G, G r2) is 0, the first depth at which the pair's D is above 0), with the median over targets; the growth rate, the least-squares slope of log2 D against depth over d = 0 to 48 of recycle 0 (depths with D above 0), in bits per block, median over targets. F: top-1 flip rate, "reduced" if the 95% interval of D_F lies below 0, "not reduced" otherwise; the same rule for the success flip rate and flips that matter, as secondary; secondary and descriptive, HW(V6f,Gf) against HWh(V6,G) on seeds 1 to 5 (HWh from the main study and follow-up 12; it separates the trunk's dtype from the matmul precision), RC(Gf) against RC(G), V6 against V6f and G against Gf, and the M1 success rates of V6f and Gf; cost, the ratio of the median process wall time (V6f to V6, Gf to G) overall and per token bucket, and the ratio of the compiled device memory and of the allocator's peak reservation per bucket, from the run records (M6, M7), with every out-of-memory stop listed. (f) Provisioning: V6 Flex-start (on demand if Flex-start has no capacity), V5 on demand, L4 on demand (not Spot: Spot preemption stopped the long Gl parts of follow-up 10, row of 2026-11-07); parts up to 8 h of job on TPU and 4 h on GPU; at most one arm per platform at a time (rows of 2026-11-04 and 2026-11-05), launched from the Mac by the chain launcher with relaunch of interrupted parts. Order: v5e L; v6e L, then V6f (r1, then the r2 subset), then P (P0 first, then the levels from 2^-5 down); L4 L (r1 and r2), then Gf (r1 and r2, `MAX_L4=8`). (g) Cost, from `cloud/prices.csv` and the main-study medians of process wall time per bucket and of overheads per part; the float32 slowdown is unknown and taken as 3 (expected) and 6 (worst, also the size of the parts): P 7 plans, 840 processes, about 28 VM h, 38 USD expected (47 worst); L 40 processes, about 4 VM h, 5 USD (7); V6f 310 processes, about 31 VM h, 42 USD (82); Gf 600 processes, about 166 VM h, 149 USD (282). Total about 234 USD expected, 417 worst. Cap 417 USD, the worst case rather than 1.2 times the expected cost, because the float32 slowdown is unmeasured; budget rule as earlier follow-ups, applied to the Google TPU Builders credit only (574.88 USD on 2026-11-10; the Free Trial upgrade credit is not counted): an arm is launched only if the balance minus the expected cost of every running arm and of this arm stays at or above the floor of 25 USD, and follow-up 13's spend with this arm stays within the cap. Download to the Mac (analysis inputs): the AlphaFold 3 outputs and run records of the 1,790 processes, about 3.5 GB (main-study median per process by bucket: 1.0, 1.8, 2.4 and 3.7 MB for 256 to 1,024 tokens), and the L capture, about 7.0 GB (computed per pilot target from its token and ligand counts), about 10.5 GB in total against 38 GB free on 2026-11-10. (h) Calendar and stop rules: the implementation is committed (and the OSF addendum registered, if Lorenzo registers one) before the first run; launch as soon as the implementation is committed (aim 2026-11-11), at the latest by 2026-11-25; every run finished by 2026-11-30 (`finish_by`), else the arm is reported as incomplete; analysis by 2026-12-05; no run dropped or selected on results; an interrupted part is relaunched unchanged; each arm passes the budget rule. Expected duration from launch: about 1 h on v5e, 24 h on L4 (43 h worst; 60 parts on 8 L4), 60 h on v6e (96 h worst), so the last run is expected about 2.5 days after launch (about 4 days at worst): about 2026-11-13 to 2026-11-14 for a launch on 2026-11-11, and before 2026-11-30 even for a launch on 2026-11-25 at the worst case. | The self-review of the paper (`review_isc_pc.md`, `review_numerics.md`) asked why the top-ranked sample flips and what avoiding it would cost |

(a) Mechanism (item a): `harness/fu13_probe.py` (new), imported by `harness/af3_entry.py` only when an `AF3_FU13_*` variable is in its environment (11 added lines: the guarded import, and the key `fu13` in `af3_entry_stats.json` only when the probe applied something), so every earlier configuration runs the same code as before. Variables: `AF3_FU13_BFLOAT16=none` sets `GlobalConfig.bfloat16` on the config `run_alphafold.make_model_config` returns (a3); `AF3_FU13_PERTURB_EPS` (0 or 2^-K) and `AF3_FU13_PERTURB_SEED` (2026) wrap `Model._sample_diffusion`, which replaces the single and pair arrays of the embeddings dict that `Model.__call__` then also hands to the confidence and distogram heads, with X + eps * (norm(m X) / norm(m Z)) * (m Z) as item a1 states, Z from `jax.random.normal` with key fold_in(fold_in(PRNGKey(2026), h), seed) split into the single and pair keys, h the first 4 bytes of the SHA-256 of the input's `name` (big-endian), seed the process's one seed (the probe refuses a process with more than one seed; every plan runs one seed per process); `AF3_FU13_CAPTURE=1` subclasses `modules.PairFormerIteration` (trunk blocks only, by module name `trunk_pairformer`) and `evoformer.Evoformer`, both looked up by AlphaFold3 when the model is built, with an ordered `jax.debug.callback` at each block input and on each recycle's Evoformer output; the host keeps recycles 0 and 10 (single at depths 0 to 48, pair at even depths on the token subset S of item a2: every real non-protein token plus 64 protein tokens at `numpy.round(numpy.linspace(0, n_protein - 1, 64))`) and the trunk output (depth 48) of every recycle, as the 16-bit patterns of the bfloat16 values, in `<run>/<target>/fu13_capture_seed-1.npz` next to `af3_output/` (so the AlphaFold3 output folder holds exactly its usual files); the file records the token count, the subset, the event counts and any ordering error. Any other `AF3_FU13_*` name, an eps other than 0 or 2^-K, or `intermediate` is refused. `cloud/af3_run.sh` uploads the whole of `harness/`, so the probe reaches every VM unchanged. | 2026-11-10 | Follow-up 13 (label `why`), fix of the layer-wise capture on the L4 and relaunch of LG, recorded before the relaunch and before any follow-up 13 output was read; not a change in what is computed. Cause (row of 2026-11-10, LG stopped): the 20 processes of `20261110T092001Z_l4_why_lg_01` exited 1 at the first capture event with "RuntimeError: jax.debug.callback failed to find a local CPU device to place the inputs on". In jax 0.10.2 the host side of `jax.debug.callback` places the callback's arguments on a local CPU device before calling it (`jax/_src/debugging.py`, `debug_callback_impl`, lines 89 to 94), and `l4_xla_fu13_capture` set `JAX_PLATFORMS=cuda`, so no CPU backend existed; on the TPU (L6, 10 capture files) the configuration sets no `JAX_PLATFORMS` and JAX initialises the CPU backend too. Fix, one line in `harness/configs.yaml`, in `l4_xla_fu13_capture` only: `JAX_PLATFORMS: cuda,cpu`. Why the computation does not move: JAX ranks the platforms of `JAX_PLATFORMS` in the order given and takes the highest as the default backend (`jax/_src/xla_bridge.py`, `backends()`, lines 816 to 844: priorities `len(platforms)` down to 1), so cuda stays the default; AlphaFold3 places the model and its inputs on `jax.local_devices(backend='gpu')` (`run_alphafold.py` line 1033, `jax.jit(..., device=...)` and `jax.device_put`), so every computation of the model runs on the GPU, and only the callback's host-side arguments, already copied to the host, sit on the CPU device; the harness probe records `default_backend` and `device_kind` in each `session.json`, and the relaunch is checked to show `gpu` and NVIDIA L4 there and in every `run.json`. One behaviour differs from `cuda` alone: with no visible NVIDIA GPU, JAX would skip cuda instead of failing (`backends()`, the `has_visible_nvidia_gpu` check); AlphaFold3's `jax.local_devices(backend='gpu')` then raises, and the VM's device check requires the L4 before the job, so no process can run on the CPU unnoticed. The order rule was also checked on the Mac with jax 0.10.2 and the jax-mps plugin (`JAX_PLATFORMS=mps,cpu`: default mps; `cpu,mps`: default cpu); the callback itself cannot be tried there (no CUDA; MPS has no `debug_callback` lowering), so the relaunch is its first run on the L4. No other configuration changes: `cpu_xla` and `l4_xla_fu13_capture` are the only ones listing cpu, and every other GPU configuration keeps `JAX_PLATFORMS=cuda` (test). `harness/plans/config_diff_fu13.py` allows `JAX_PLATFORMS=cuda,cpu` for LG only and compares it as `cuda` in the rows without the follow-up 13 settings: 0 settings differ beyond the allowed ones, all 18 such rows the same. Plans unchanged (`make_fu13_plans.py --check` up to date; the earlier generators up to date). Tests: `analysis/tests/test_fu13.py` (the L4 capture is the only GPU configuration listing cpu, cuda first; the LG relaunch gate), `analysis/.venv/bin/python -m pytest -q analysis/tests scripts/release -p no:cacheprovider`: 877 passed, 2 skipped. Files (SHA-256): `harness/configs.yaml` 77bc9443c8a0c8b8335b16773f1a4c48094c83d85eb99ed413ea97d240b9fdd3, `harness/plans/config_diff_fu13.py` 318e47d30a2f240e9e69e8d40ec70a6eab0a3bed50da063245512ea2a4fd8d77, `harness/plans/config_diff_fu13.md` 9b796bd0c873970bdbbd2fcf9e3272aca0319fb2560d938472d57c02ba56513c, `scripts/monitor/lg_relaunch_gate.sh` 05a55383b0ba0ae42ec21700fc91fbae83f7ce805b9b66cf5c91b0dbd1cb586b, `analysis/tests/test_fu13.py` de561735734596b732014e99ab12a3fce34b1491225ca7740016b71c7dd03713. Relaunch of LG, otherwise unchanged: the same plan `harness/plans/why/why_lg_01.yaml` (10 pilot targets, seed 1, r1 and r2, one part), the same parts file `results/parts_lg.txt`, on demand; the failed session `20261110T092001Z_l4_why_lg_01` and its 20 processes are kept as they are (results folder and bucket), recorded here, and not listed in `scripts/analysis_runs/fu13/sessions.txt`, which lists the complete session of the relaunch. | 2026-11-12 | Follow-up experiment 14, the float32 trunk on every accelerator (label `f32`), specified after the main-study and follow-up 1 to 13 outcomes were read (the last of them the follow-up 13 analysis row of 2026-11-12) and before any run; reported as a follow-up, outside the pre-registered study, whatever it shows. For the sizing only run records were read (process wall time, compile time, device memory and per-part overheads of the main study, follow-ups 3, 9 and 13 and the memory-limit measurements); every outcome value quoted here is one of an earlier row. Questions. (F) Follow-up 13 found that with AlphaFold 3's trunk in float32 at the highest matmul precision, TPU v6e and the L4 rank the same sample first in 299 of 300 (target, seed) predictions (HW(V6f,Gf) top-1 flip 0.3% [0.0, 1.0]; D_F -13.0 [-17.7, -8.3], reduced). Does this hold for the other accelerators of the study, TPU v5e, the A100 80 GB and the H100 80 GB: does the float32 trunk make every pair of accelerators agree on the top-ranked sample, and at what cost in time and memory on each? (D) Does XLA's deterministic-ops flag make recompilation bit-identical on the A100 and the H100, as on the L4 (RC(Gd) 0 flips with every sample bit-identical, main study), and at what cost? (a) Settings. The float32 trunk is follow-up 13's (item a3 of its row): AlphaFold 3's `GlobalConfig.bfloat16` set to `none` by `harness/fu13_probe.py` (`AF3_FU13_BFLOAT16=none`, through `harness/af3_entry.py`) together with `JAX_DEFAULT_MATMUL_PRECISION=highest`, so the trunk and the confidence head's Pairformer run on float32 activations, with six-pass bfloat16 matmuls on TPU v5e (as on v6e) and IEEE float32 matmuls without TensorFloat-32 on the A100 and the H100 (as on the L4); no AlphaFold 3 file, no file of `af3_tpu/`, `src/`, `cloud/`, `inputs/`, `targets/` and no harness module changes. Every arm keeps the main-study settings of its bfloat16 source arm (V5 of the main study, A of follow-up 3, H of follow-up 9): AlphaFold 3 v3.0.4 with `af3_tpu/af3_tpu.patch`, the same weights, software stacks (jax 0.10.2; libtpu 0.0.43.2 on TPU; jax-cuda12-plugin and -pjrt 0.10.2 on GPU), frozen inputs of `inputs/manifest.csv`, 10 recycles, 5 samples, machine types and image families (`v5litepod-1` through the legacy TPU API, `a2-ultragpu-1g`, `a3-highgpu-1g`), XLA attention, the XLA flags and memory settings of `a100_xla` and `h100_xla`, the Eigen archive (every plan since follow-up 7), one fresh process per (target, seed, repetition) with an empty compile cache. Configurations: V5f runs `tpu_xla_fu13_f32` unchanged (V6f's configuration; the condition is read from the configuration and the device); new entries appended to `harness/configs.yaml`, none changed: `a100_xla_fu14_f32` and `h100_xla_fu14_f32` (`a100_xla` and `h100_xla` plus `AF3_FU13_BFLOAT16=none` and `JAX_DEFAULT_MATMUL_PRECISION=highest`, as `l4_xla_fu13_f32` is `l4_xla` plus them), `a100_xla_det` and `h100_xla_det` (`a100_xla` and `h100_xla` with `--xla_gpu_deterministic_ops=true` appended to `XLA_FLAGS`, as `l4_xla_det` is `l4_xla` with it). (b) Arms. (b1) Float32: V5f (TPU v5e), the 60 targets, seeds 1 to 5, r1, and r2 of the 10 pilot targets, seed 1 (check 5); Af (A100), the 60 targets, seeds 1 to 5, r1; Hf (H100), the 60 targets, seeds 1 to 5, r1. V6f and Gf are follow-up 13's, as they are (its session list `scripts/analysis_runs/fu13/sessions.txt`); RC(Gf) is follow-up 13's (60 targets, seeds 1 to 5). No r2 is run for Af and Hf and V5f r2 is not extended: no reading of item e needs the recompilation of a float32 condition (item d: where it is not measured its term is set to 0, which can only make "reduced" harder to reach). The CPU is not run: the float32 trunk on the 60 targets and five seeds would take about 470 VM h at the bfloat16 CPU times of follow-up 4 (median process 1,687, 4,050, 7,922 and 15,009 s for buckets 256 to 1,024 on `n2-highmem-16`), about 740 VM h at follow-up 13's ratio of 1.58, or 150 to 240 USD on Spot and 545 to 860 USD on demand; one 1,024-token target with five seeds alone takes 21 to 33 h on one VM, and with the four CPU VMs used so far the arm would take 5 to 8 days; the CPU is not an accelerator, and its bfloat16 reference exists only for seeds 1 and 2 (follow-up 4). (b2) Deterministic flag: Ad (A100, `a100_xla_det`) and Hd (H100, `h100_xla_det`), the 10 pilot targets, seeds 1 to 5, r1 and r2 in the same part (one VM, as the main study's RC); trunk in bfloat16 as in the main study; launched only after every float32 session has ended, under its own budget rule (item g). Out-of-memory stops and other failures are recorded with their cause, never hidden or rerun with other settings; a (target, seed) missing on either side of a contrast leaves it (section 8) and the counts are reported. (c) Checks, before any result is read: (1) every process exits 0 or its failure is recorded; (2) one machine type per condition; (3) completeness per condition, repetition and seed; (4) the settings reached every process: `af3_entry_stats.json` records `global_config_bfloat16: none` in every V5f, Af and Hf process and in no Ad or Hd process, `session.json` records `JAX_DEFAULT_MATMUL_PRECISION=highest` for the float32 arms and `--xla_gpu_deterministic_ops=true` in `XLA_FLAGS` for Ad and Hd, and every process names the expected device (TPU v5 lite, NVIDIA A100-SXM4-80GB, NVIDIA H100 80GB HBM3); (5) V5f r2 against V5f r1, the 10 pilot targets, seed 1, bit-identical in every output file (`contrasts.outputs_identical`, the mmCIF timestamp line masked), reported; a difference does not extend V5f r2 and leaves the TPU pair's D_F read as the bound of item d, which the report then states; (6) memory: the compiled device memory (peak plus arguments) per bucket of V5f, Af and Hf against the device limit (15.75, 75.29 and 75.22 GiB), every out-of-memory stop listed. Expected margin, from run records only: at 1,024 tokens the bfloat16 processes need 7.28 GiB on v5e and 7.21 and 6.96 GiB on the A100 and the H100; follow-up 13's float32 trunk raised the compiled memory at 1,024 tokens by a factor of 1.15 on v6e and 1.31 on the L4, so about 8.4 to 9.5 GiB, within the v5e's 15.75 GiB (the v5e ran 1,536 tokens at 13.47 GiB in the memory-limit measurement of 2026-10-14) and about 13% of the A100's and H100's memory (both ran 5,120 tokens in bfloat16 at 74.2 and 69.7 GiB, memory-limit measurement on A and H of 2026-11-10). (d) Statistics: as section 5, the target-clustered bootstrap with 10,000 replicates and seed 2026, every contrast paired on (target, seed) with the per-seed top-1 (M2), seeds 1 to 5 unless stated. Pairs: all ten pairs of {V5f, V6f, Gf, Af, Hf}. Primary for D_F, the pairs that carry the main study's and the follow-ups' questions: HW(V5f,V6f) (two TPU generations, as HW(V5,V6)), HW(V6f,Gf) (TPU against GPU; follow-up 13's), HW(Hf,Af) (two GPU generations, follow-up 9's headline HW(H,A)) and HW(Gf,Af) (the L4 against the A100, follow-up 3's HW(G,A)); the other six, HW(V5f,Gf), HW(V5f,Af), HW(Hf,V5f), HW(V6f,Af), HW(Hf,V6f) and HW(Hf,Gf), are secondary for D_F's descriptive counterpart and enter the "agree" rule of item e on equal terms. Statistics of each pair: the top-1 flip rate (primary), the success flip rate and flips that matter (secondary), M4 and M5 medians (`contrasts.summarize`). Against bfloat16 at the default precision, for each of the four primary pairs (P,Q): D_F = [HW(Pf,Qf) minus RC(Rf)] minus [HW(P,Q) minus RC(R)], on the same resampled targets, where R is the condition whose recompilation the earlier row subtracts: V5 for (V5,V6) (RC(V5) = 0, main study), G for (V6,G) (follow-up 13), H for (H,A) (follow-up 9's HW(H,A) minus RC(H)), G for (G,A) (follow-up 3's HW(G,A) minus RC(G)); bfloat16 terms from the seed pairs of the main study, follow-up 3 and follow-up 9. RC(Gf) is measured (follow-up 13); RC(V5f) and RC(Hf) are not measured on the 60 targets, and their term is set to 0. A flip rate is never negative, so in every bootstrap replicate D_F with that term set to 0 is at least D_F with the term measured; its 95% upper limit is therefore at or above D_F's, and "reduced" read from it implies "reduced" for D_F. On a TPU the term is 0 whenever recompilation is bit-identical, as in every earlier TPU arm (RC(V5) and RC(V6): every sample bit-identical; V6f r2: 10 of 10), which check 5 tests on V5f. Against bfloat16 at the highest matmul precision, where both conditions of the pair have such an arm (descriptive): HW(V6f,Gf) minus HWh(V6,G) on seeds 1 to 5 (main study and follow-up 12), HW(Gf,Af) minus HWh(G,A) on seeds 1 and 2 (Gh of the main study and Ah of follow-up 3; HWh(G,A) is computed here for the first time), and HW(V6f,Af) minus HWh(V6,A) on seeds 1 and 2 (follow-up 3); none exists for (V5,V6) and (H,A) (no V5h or Hh arm), reported as not available. Also descriptive: HW(Pf,Qf) minus HW(P,Q) for all ten pairs (every bfloat16 pair exists in the main study and follow-ups 3 and 9); F(V5) = V5 against V5f, F(A) and F(H), the top-1 flips between bfloat16 and float32 on one chip; the per-seed top-1 success rate (M1) of V5f, Af and Hf beside V5, A and H, and the success flip rate and flips that matter of the ten pairs, also on the 39 post-cutoff targets. Expected precision, from earlier rows: the bfloat16 references HW(P,Q) minus RC(R) for the top-1 flip rate are 18.0 [14.3, 21.7] (V5,V6), 13.3 [8.7, 18.0] (V6,G), 11.3 [6.3, 16.0] (H,A) and 4.7 [0.3, 9.3] (G,A) percentage points; with a float32 rate near 0 the half-width of D_F is about 5 points, so (V5,V6) and (H,A) can read reduced, while for (G,A) D_F cannot fall below about -4.7 and will rarely read reduced even if Gf and Af agree; the "agree" rule carries that pair. (e) Reading rules, fixed now. (1) Reduced: for each of the four primary pairs, the top-1 flip rate is "reduced" if the 95% interval of D_F (or of its bound, item d, named in the report) lies below 0, "not reduced" otherwise; the same rule for the success flip rate and flips that matter, as secondary. (2) Agree: for each of the ten pairs, "agree" if the upper limit of the 95% interval of the float32 top-1 flip rate HW(Pf,Qf) is below 5.0 percentage points, "agreement not shown" otherwise; success flips and flips that matter are reported with their intervals, without a threshold. The threshold of 5.0 points was chosen after the follow-up 13 analysis row of 2026-11-12 was read, and before any follow-up 14 output exists, for these reasons: it lies below the top-1 flip rate of every bfloat16 GPU against its own recompilation (RC(G) 7.3%, RC(H) 9.3%, RC(A) 10.0%), so "agree" means that two accelerators agree more closely than one bfloat16 GPU agrees with itself, and far below every bfloat16 cross-platform rate (12.0% for HW(G,A) to 23.3% for HW(H,V5)); it lies at the lowest point of follow-up 13's perturbation curve (5.0% [1.7, 9.2] at 2^-11, 10.0% at 2^-14), so an upper limit below it means the top-1 choice moves less than under a random perturbation of the trunk output of any size that follow-up 13 tested; and it can be reached with 300 (target, seed) on 60 targets: a true rate of 1 to 2% gives an upper limit of about 2.5 to 4% with the main study's clustering. Follow-up 13's HW(V6f,Gf) (0.3% [0.0, 1.0]) was known when the threshold was chosen; every threshold from 1.0 point up reads that pair the same way, and its reading is reported as known at specification, not as new evidence. (3) Generality: follow-up 13's F result is stated as holding "on every accelerator of the study" only if all ten pairs of {V5f, V6f, Gf, Af, Hf} read "agree", each at its own 95% level (an intersection-union test, so no multiplicity correction is applied); otherwise the pairs that agree and those that do not are named, and the sentence is not written. (4) Cost, descriptive: as follow-up 13's `f_cost.csv`, the ratio of the median process wall time (V5f to V5, Af to A, Hf to H; V5, A and H from their r1 processes) overall and per token bucket, the ratio of the compiled device memory per bucket, and on the TPU of the allocator's peak reservation; every out-of-memory stop listed. (5) Deterministic flag (item b2), on each of the A100 and the H100: "recompilation bit-identical with the flag" if every one of the 50 (target, seed) of Ad (or Hd) r2 is identical to r1 in every output file (`contrasts.outputs_identical`, the mmCIF timestamp line masked; the strict check of Gd in the main study), otherwise the number that differ is reported; RC(Ad) and RC(Hd), top-1 flip, success flip and flips that matter, with intervals, beside RC(A) and RC(H) on the same 10 targets and seeds; DET(A) = A r1 against Ad r1 and DET(H) = H r1 against Hd r1, descriptive, as DET in the main study; cost, the ratio of the median process wall time per bucket, Ad to A and Hd to H on the pilot targets (different VMs, as DET). (f) Provisioning and launch, for the shortest time to the last result; not a change in any computation. Quotas read on 2026-11-12 (read-only): the legacy TPU API's `TPUV5sLitepodPerProjectPerZoneForTPUAPI` 512 in europe-west4-b (and in the other zones of europe-west4 and us-central1); GPUS_ALL_REGIONS 10; `NVIDIA-A100-80GB-GPUS-per-project-region` (on demand) and `PREEMPTIBLE-NVIDIA-A100-80GB-GPUS-per-project-region` (Spot, requested and approved on 2026-11-12, Google Cloud e-mails of 19:25 to 19:27 UTC) both us-central1 2, us-east4 1, us-east5 1, europe-west4 1; `PREEMPTIBLE-NVIDIA-H100-GPUS-per-project-region` 64 in every region (a Cloud Quotas entry, which `gcloud compute regions describe` does not list); no on-demand H100 quota; no `af3-run` VM running. v5e on demand, not Spot (a preempted v5e part is lost and relaunched whole), in europe-west4-b with at most four VMs at a time (`MAX_V5E=4`); the legacy TPU API takes one zone per launcher, so a part that finds no capacity there may be relaunched unchanged in europe-west4-a, which offers `v5litepod-1` (`gcloud compute tpus tpu-vm accelerator-types list`, 2026-11-12; europe-west4-c offers no TPU type), from a prepared parts file, recorded in a row. The A100 on Spot (`SPOT=1`, as Hf), `a2-ultragpu-1g` in the launcher's A100 zones with the regional caps us-central1 2, us-east5 1, europe-west4 1 and us-east4 1 (the Spot quota); on demand only as a fallback: an Af part that has not obtained a Spot VM within 2 h of the wrapper's start is launched on demand from a prepared parts file (`PROVISIONING=standard` per part, the same plans), after a row; the launcher needs no change for either. The H100 on Spot, as follow-up 9 and the memory-limit and warm-cost measurements on H. A preempted part is relaunched unchanged. Item b2's parts on Spot on both GPUs. The provisioning model and the zone are recorded per session and enter no contrast, as for follow-up 13 (rows of 2026-11-12). Three wrappers start at once, one per platform (`~/Desktop/af3_drafts/retry_arm_par.sh` with `ALLOW_OTHER_LAUNCHERS=1`): v5e (`MAX_V5E=4`), the A100 (`MAX_A100=5`) and the H100 (`MAX_H100=5`; with the A100's 5, the GPUS_ALL_REGIONS quota of 10); item b2's wrapper starts after the float32 wrappers have ended. Parts hold one target's runs (r1 and r2 of Ad and Hd together), as many parts per arm as a multiple of the platform's VMs at a time (V5f 8, Af 15, Hf 10, Ad 5, Hd 5, V5f r2 1), balanced longest first, each at most 8 h (TPU) or 4 h (GPU) of job at the worst factor; deadline ceil(worst job min x 1.25 + 20); `FETCH=full` for V5f r2, Ad and Hd, whose outputs check 5 and reading 5 compare byte for byte (V5f r1, seed 1, of the pilot targets copied from the bucket before the analysis). The launches run under `caffeinate -dims` from the main checkout at the commit that merges the implementation. (g) Cost and budget. From `cloud/prices.csv` and the bfloat16 medians of process wall time per bucket of the source arms (V5 71, 104, 163 and 262 s; A 102, 129, 169 and 232 s; H 65, 75, 98 and 137 s for buckets 256 to 1,024) and their per-part overheads, times a factor: float32, expected 1.58 on every platform (follow-up 13's ratio of median wall time: V6f 1.58, Gf 1.55); worst 3.0 on v5e, 3.8 on the A100 and 3.6 on the H100. The worst factors follow from the share p of the bfloat16 time spent in matmuls, with the slowdown 1 + p (r - 1) when matmuls become r times slower: on the L4, r is about 4 (float32 against bfloat16 peak) and the measured 1.55 gives p = 0.18 (0.24 at 1,024 tokens); on the v6e, r = 6 (six passes) and 1.58 gives p = 0.12; with the L4's p, the A100 (r about 16) gives 3.75 and the H100 (r about 15) 3.57, rounded up to 3.8 and 3.6; the v5e (r = 6) gives at most 2.2, so its worst stays 3.0. The highest-precision arm of follow-up 3 suggests the A100 is less matmul-bound than the L4: the float32 matmuls that move from TensorFloat-32 to IEEE float32 (r about 8 on the A100, 2 on the L4) raised the median wall by 2 to 11% per bucket on the A100 (Ah against A) and by 5 to 10% on the L4 (Gh against G), so the A100's matmul share is at most about 0.3 times the L4's, which gives about 2.1 on the A100 and 2.0 on the H100; the worst factors keep the larger values. Deterministic arms: 1.12 expected (Gd against G in the main study, 1.12, 1.10, 1.05 and 1.03 by bucket), 1.5 worst. Prices, the highest row of each platform among the launcher's regions: v5e on demand 1.56 USD per chip hour (lifetime rounded up to whole hours), A100 Spot 3.02 USD/h (us-central1; on demand 5.66, us-east4), H100 Spot 6.59 USD/h (us-east4; on demand 12.37). Float32 arms: V5f with r2 310 processes, 20.0 VM h, 31.21 USD expected (55.75 worst); Af 300 processes, 23.9 VM h, 71.81 USD (151.92; on demand 134.80 and 285.22); Hf 300 processes, 14.8 VM h, 97.49 USD (192.41): 200.51 USD expected, 400.08 worst (lifetime bound 604.67, reached only if every part ran to its deadline); at the factors that follow-up 3's highest-precision arm suggests (about 2.1 and 2.0) about 240 USD. Item b2: Ad 100 processes, 6.5 VM h, 19.47 USD (24.41); Hd 100 processes, 4.6 VM h, 30.32 USD (36.66): 49.79 USD expected, 61.07 worst. Budget, on the Google TPU Builders credit only, 326.57 USD read on 2026-11-12 (the Free Trial credit, 257.47 EUR, is not counted: it did not decrease during follow-up 13's spending, so it probably does not cover these SKUs). Cap of the float32 arms: 300 USD (the balance minus the floor of 25 USD); cap of item b2: 62 USD (its worst case). Budget rule before each float32 wrapper (`scripts/monitor/credit_check_fu14.sh v5e|a100|h100 BALANCE`): GO if the balance read from the console, minus what the other running float32 wrappers still need, minus this wrapper's expected cost, stays at or above 25 USD, and the projected float32 spend with this wrapper stays within 300 USD. Stop rule, before each A100 part (`scripts/monitor/fu14_guarded_launch.sh`, the A100 wrapper's `LAUNCHER`, which runs `scripts/monitor/fu14_budget.py guard` and then `cloud/af3_run.sh` unchanged): the projected spend of the float32 arms is the spend so far from the session records (VM running to deletion at the list price of its provisioning) plus what every float32 part still needs (its expected cost, less what a running session of it has spent; the v5e and H100 parts not started at their expected cost) plus this part's expected cost (at the on-demand price for an on-demand fallback); if it exceeds 300 USD, the part is not launched (no VM is created), the wrapper starts no further part (its STOP file), the reason is written to `results/f32_cap_stop.txt`, every later A100 part is refused while that file exists, and Lorenzo decides in a row whether to raise the cap or stop; running parts finish. The v5e and H100 wrappers are not stopped by this rule: their worst cases together, 248.16 USD, are within the cap, and the guard counts what they still need before every A100 part. Item b2 (`credit_check_fu14.sh det BALANCE`, before its wrapper): GO only if no float32 session is still running, the balance read then is at least item b2's worst case plus 25 USD (86.07 USD), and item b2's spend with its worst case stays within 62 USD. Every gate also refuses a launch after 2026-11-18 or an expected end after 2026-11-20 (item h). Download to the Mac (analysis inputs): the outputs and run records of the light fetch, about 2 GB (main-study median per process by bucket: 1.0, 1.8, 2.4 and 3.7 MB), and the full outputs compared byte for byte, about 6 GB (follow-up 13's full copies: about 28 MB per process), against 24 GiB free on 2026-11-12. (h) Duration and calendar. From the first VM to the last result of each wrapper, at the expected factor and without capacity waits: v5e 5.6 h (9.9 h worst) with four VMs at a time; A100 4.8 h (10.1 h); H100 3.0 h (5.9 h); then item b2, A100 1.4 h (1.8 h) and H100 1.0 h (1.2 h). Known capacity risks: A100 80 GB on demand had up to 94 attempts without capacity for one part in follow-up 3 (2026-10-17), and Spot A100 capacity has not been tried in this project; v5e in europe-west4-b had no capacity for 2 h 20 min before L5 of follow-up 13 started (19 attempts, 2026-11-10); the H100 on Spot ran without preemption in follow-up 9 and in the memory-limit and warm-cost measurements. The implementation is committed and the OSF addendum registered before the first run; launch aim 2026-11-13, so that the last float32 result is expected the same night and item b2 on 2026-11-14, with the analysis on 2026-11-14 or 2026-11-15, before the 2026-11-17 aim; hard limits: launch by 2026-11-18, every run finished by 2026-11-20 (`launch_by` and `finish_by` in every plan), else the arm is reported as incomplete; analysis by 2026-11-22. No run is dropped or selected on results; an interrupted part is relaunched unchanged; every launch, relaunch, fallback and stop is recorded in section 10. | Follow-up 13's float32 result was measured on one TPU and one GPU; the paper's recommendation needs it on every accelerator of the study |

At 01:48Z the guard (`scripts/monitor/fu14_guarded_launch.sh`) refused `f32_af_15` with "projected float32 spend 309.88 > 300 USD" and wrote `results/f32_cap_stop.txt`; from then on `f32_af_13` (preempted again at 00:46Z) and `f32_af_15` were refused without any VM being created, and the A100 relaunch wrapper of the supervisor used its 12 rounds that way by about 06:08Z. Cause: in `scripts/monitor/fu14_budget.py`, `events()` read the launcher's exit code from the line "launcher: exit code N" as the first word after "exit" ("code") instead of the last (N), so no session counted as complete and `still_needed()` counted the expected cost of every completed H100 and v5e part again (about 120 USD). The synthetic test (`analysis/tests/test_fu14.py`) wrote "launcher: exit N" without "code", so the tests passed. With the code read correctly: 29 complete sessions, float32 spend 182.25 USD, projection for `f32_af_15` 189.53 USD against the cap of 300 USD, which is unchanged. Correction: one line (`[0]` to `[-1]`) and the test now writes the real format "launcher: exit code N"; committed with this row; the marker is then removed by Lorenzo, and the supervisor relaunches `f32_af_13` and `f32_af_15` unchanged (its A100 relaunch round 2 of 4), through the guard as before; item b2 follows as in the supervision row. Cause: `analysis/fu14.py` builds the references A_pilot and H_pilot (the r1 processes of A, follow-up 3, and H, follow-up 9, on the 10 pilot targets, seeds 1 to 5, as item e 5 specifies) as copies of A and H rows, then removed duplicate processes with `processes()`, whose key was (session, target, seed) without the condition, so every A_pilot and H_pilot row was dropped as a duplicate of its A or H row; the 50 + 50 reference processes were in the input (the sessions of follow-ups 3 and 9 that the analysis already reads). | 2026-11-14 | Post hoc analysis of frozen outputs, returned structures across platforms (label `returned`), specified after the main-study and follow-up 1 to 14 outcomes were read and before any value of this analysis was computed; descriptive whatever it shows, outside the pre-registered study. (a) Question: does the platform change the quality of the structure AlphaFold 3 returns? For each paired prediction (target, seed) of a contrast, the top-ranked sample of side A and the top-ranked sample of side B are each compared with the experimental structure, and the two qualities are differenced. (b) Data, all frozen: the per-sample tables and seed pairs of the main study (`results/analysis/main_20261014/primary_v2`, `samples.csv` and `contrasts/pairs_seed.csv`), follow-up 4 (`cpu60_20261018`), follow-up 13 (`fu13_20261112/samples.csv`, V6f and Gf) and follow-up 14 (`fu14_20261113b`). No session is collected and no output is rescored. Contrasts: the three primary pairs HW(V5,V6), HW(V6,G) and HW(G,C) of the main study (HW(G,C) on the 6 targets and seeds 1 and 2 that C ran); HW(G,C) on the 60 targets of follow-up 4; the ten float32 pairs HW(Pf,Qf) of follow-up 14 (four primary, six secondary); as yardsticks, RC(G) and SD(V6) of the main study and RC(Gf). Sides as registered: HW(P,Q) is P r1 against Q r1 on the same seed, RC(P) is r1 against r2, and SD(P) is seed s against seed s' of P r1. The top-1 of each side is the registered one (`top1_a`, `top1_b` of `pairs_seed.csv`). (c) Measures: the top-1 sample's M1 ligand RMSD from the experimental structure (`m1_ligand_rmsd`, angstrom, after pocket superposition), its ipTM and its mean pLDDT, each as the paired difference d = B minus A, A the first-named side. A unit without the measure on either side leaves that measure's statistics and is counted. Strata: every paired unit ("all", primary) and the units whose registered top-1 differs ("flipped", beside it; not for SD, whose sample indices are unrelated draws). (d) Statistics: median of d (primary) and mean of d, each with a 95% percentile interval from the target bootstrap (`analysis/contrasts.boot_ci`: targets resampled with replacement, the units of a drawn target pooled, 10,000 replicates, a fresh generator from seed 2026 for every statistic, so every statistic sees the same resampled targets); also the median of the absolute d. No correction for multiple comparisons. (e) Reading rule, ligand RMSD, median of d, stratum "all" (the "flipped" stratum is read the same way, beside it): the platform returns poses farther from the experimental structure on B if the interval lies above 0, closer if below 0, and "not distinguishable" otherwise; in addition, "within 0.1 A" if the whole interval lies in [-0.1, 0.1] A. The band is 5% of the 2 A success threshold, chosen here before any value. ipTM and mean pLDDT: the same three-way reading, descriptive, without a band. (f) Checks (the analysis stops if 1 or 2 fails): 1, every registered top-1 names exactly one row of the per-sample table; 2, that row is the argmax of the full-precision ranking score of its (condition, repetition, target, seed) under `contrasts.top`, and its `m1_success` equals the registered success; 3, reported, the units without each measure per contrast and side. (g) Already seen before this row: every registered result of the main study and follow-ups 1 to 14, including M4 (RMSD between the two sides' structures, with the reading in the paper that "the predicted structures barely moved"), the success flips and flips that matter, and the per-platform top-1 success rates (Q3, follow-up 14's success table). The paired difference of the RMSD from the experimental structure, ipTM and pLDDT between the two sides' top-1 has not been computed or looked at. To prepare this row, the agent read the code and the column headers of the input tables. A structure check printed only, per contrast, the number of seed pairs and whether both sides exist in the per-sample table, and no value. (h) Post hoc status: asked by the reviewers after the results; descriptive; it cannot change a registered reading and is reported whatever it shows. (i) Code: `analysis/returned_structures.py`, tests `analysis/tests/test_returned_structures.py` (9 synthetic tests; the seed pairs built by the registered `followup34.build`), `scripts/analysis_runs/returned/reproduce.sh` (tests first, then the analysis), at commit `8e64587` (branch `paper`). Run from the main checkout: `DATE=YYYYMMDD bash scripts/analysis_runs/returned/reproduce.sh`. Outputs in `results/analysis/returned_DATE/`: `units.csv`, `quality.csv`, `checks.csv`, `inputs.tsv` (SHA-256 of the inputs), `report.md`, `run.log`, `analysis_commit.txt`. | The reviewers asked whether a change of platform changes the quality of what AlphaFold 3 returns, not only which sample it returns. The registered measures answer this only through the binary success and the RMSD between the two sides. |
| 2026-11-14 | Post hoc sensitivity analysis of registered readings, rare-event intervals (label `rare`), specified after the main-study and follow-up 1 to 14 outcomes were read and before any value of this analysis was computed; outside the pre-registered study. The registered readings stay as they are. This analysis only says whether each would change under an interval suited to few events. (a) Question: for readings that rest on few events, would the reading change if the 95% interval were bias-corrected and accelerated? (b) Readings, in seven families, every row of a family re-read whatever its events (all targets of each folder; the post-cutoff subsets are not re-read). F1, main study, primary pairs HW(V5,V6), HW(V6,G) and HW(G,C) minus RC(P), and minus RC(Q) as secondary, for the success flip and flips that matter (`primary_v2/contrasts/hw_minus_rc.csv`). F2, follow-up 4, HW(G,C) on 60 targets minus RC(G), the same two flips (`cpu60_20261018`). F3, follow-up 11, HW(V5,V6) and HW(V6,G) minus RC(P) and RC(Q), the same two flips (its secondary readings; `repl_20261106`, targets "all"). F4, follow-up 12, HWh(V6,G) minus RC(V6) and RC(G), all three flips, at seeds 1 and 2 (`primary_v2`) and at seeds 1 to 5 (`prec5_20261107`), and HWh(G,C) minus RC(G) at seeds 1 and 2. F5, follow-ups 13 and 14, D_F of the four primary float32 pairs, success flip and flips that matter (`fu14_20261113b/d_f.csv`; an unmeasured RCf is a zero term, as registered). F6, follow-up 14, the top-1 flip rate of the ten float32 pairs (the "agree" reading; `fu14_20261113b/contrasts.csv`). F7, the zero-event statements: RC(V5) and RC(V6) of the main study, and RC(Gf), RC(V5f), RC(Ad) and RC(Hd) of follow-up 14, all three flips, with no reading rule. Each row also states whether it meets the reviewers' criterion: fewer than 10 events on a term, or a registered lower limit of 0.0 or 0.3 points after rounding to one decimal. Rows that do not meet it are reported too. (c) Method: BCa (bias-corrected and accelerated; Efron, JASA 82:171-185, 1987) on the registered target bootstrap. The replicates are the registered ones: the same 10,000 draws of targets from seed 2026 over the same sorted common targets, and the same pooled rates (the sum of the flips over the units of the drawn targets divided by their number). The percentile interval is therefore recomputed first, and it must equal the registered one within 1e-9; a row that does not is marked "not reproduced" and is not re-read. Bias correction z0 = Phi^-1(p), p = (number of replicates below the estimate + half the number equal to it) / 10,000; acceleration a from the jackknife over targets (leave one target out); limits at the replicate percentiles Phi(z0 + (z0 + z)/(1 - a(z0 + z))) for z = -1.96 and 1.96, with numpy's linear interpolation, as the registered intervals. If every replicate equals the estimate (no event on any term), the interval is the estimate and the row says so. If p is 0 or 1, BCa is undefined and the row says so. Beside it, for single rates only, the two-sided 95% Clopper-Pearson upper limit on the fraction of targets with at least one event. It bounds the per-prediction rate from above whatever the correlation within a target, so it is conservative; it is reported, not read. (d) Why BCa and not an exact target-level method: BCa keeps the registered estimand, the clustering by target and the registered draws, so a change of reading comes from how the limits are taken and from nothing else. It corrects the bias and skewness of the replicate distribution, which are largest when events are few (a rate with a handful of events is discrete, right-skewed and bounded at 0), where the percentile interval is only first-order accurate. An exact target-level method answers a different question. It bounds the fraction of targets with an event, not the per-prediction rate, and it gives no interval on the same scale for the paired differences HW minus RC and D_F. At 60 targets its upper limit with no event is 5.96 points, above follow-up 14's 5-point threshold, so the "agree" rule could not be met by any data. Limits of BCa, stated before any value: it is undefined or degenerate without events, and with few events in few targets (HW(G,C) of the main study has 6 targets) the jackknife acceleration is coarse. (e) Reading rule: each family's registered rule applied to the BCa interval. Beyond recompilation if the lower limit is above 0 (F1 to F4); agree if the upper limit is below 5.0 points (F6); reduced if the upper limit is below 0 (F5); F7 none. A reading "changes" if the BCa reading differs from the registered reading. The report gives the number of readings that change and lists them. The paper may say how many would change. It does not replace a registered reading. No correction for multiple comparisons. (f) Already seen before this row: every registered interval and reading of the seven families (they are in the paper and its supplement), including their event counts; the choice of the families follows the reviewers' criterion, which was set after seeing that these readings rest on few events. No BCa interval and no Clopper-Pearson bound has been computed. To prepare this row, the agent read the code and the column headers of the registered tables. A structure check confirmed only that every registered row named in (b) exists exactly once, 71 rows: F1 10, F2 2, F3 8, F4 15, F5 8, F6 10, F7 18. It printed no value. (g) Post hoc status: asked by the reviewers after the results; a sensitivity analysis; the registered readings stand whatever it shows, and it is reported whatever it shows. (h) Code: `analysis/rare_intervals.py`, tests `analysis/tests/test_rare_intervals.py` (15 synthetic tests; the registered tables of the test are built with the registered `contrasts.hw_minus_rc`, `contrasts.summarize` and `fu14.d_f_table`, and every one is reproduced), `scripts/analysis_runs/rare/reproduce.sh` (tests first, then the analysis), at commit `8e64587` (branch `paper`). Run from the main checkout: `DATE=YYYYMMDD bash scripts/analysis_runs/rare/reproduce.sh`. Outputs in `results/analysis/rare_DATE/`: `intervals.csv`, `inputs.tsv` (SHA-256 of the inputs), `report.md`, `run.log`, `analysis_commit.txt`. | The reviewers asked whether percentile intervals over 60 targets are reliable for readings with fewer than 10 events or a lower limit at 0.0 or 0.3 points (follow-up 11's secondary readings, follow-up 12 at two seeds, and the primary success flips and flips that matter). |

Output `results/analysis/returned_20261114/` (`report.md` written at 14:55Z); SHA-256: `report.md` `90624bcb01a7c096a9c8124a136f4f40409e90b9084ce32b3aa99398ba06b06f`, `quality.csv` `d2f31cfc5ed71c51995082dcd82a751be872fe857ef9046744401558495ed576`, `units.csv` `6386d855fa2b473e23b437d53e6135cab5a6ef0f2461304bebecc69c6d17f78f`, `checks.csv` `b1bfef99d534391910e5507f8b966226501b8cf5b42dab6be62a340780262d9a`, `inputs.tsv` `08c7d60a86e2f27e7ddec909a9a88d29278d3704f92a2c0c39a4ffeae04d119b` (the SHA-256 of the seven input tables). Checks: (1) and (2) pass: every registered top-1 of the 17 contrasts, 4,932 paired units, names exactly one row of its per-sample table, is the argmax of the full-precision ranking score of its (condition, repetition, target, seed), and has the registered success (the analysis stops otherwise, and it completed). (3) Units without the ligand RMSD on both sides: 15 of 300 in each 60-target contrast (the 14 contrasts HW(V5,V6), HW(V6,G), RC(G), the ten float32 pairs and RC(Gf)), 30 of 600 in SD(V6), 6 of 120 in HW(G,C) on 60 targets and 0 of 12 in HW(G,C) of the main study. They are the three glycan targets 7OZC, 7WDT and 7WJB (five seeds each; two in follow-up 4), whose M1 was never computed: AlphaFold 3 v3.0.4 removes atom O1 from ligands on its glycan lists at its default, so no predicted ligand matches the reference (row of 2026-10-14; `analysis/score_ligand.py`, `af3_removed_atoms`). The main study's HW(G,C) has none of the three among its 6 targets. ipTM and mean pLDDT are present for every unit. Results, read with the rule of item e (d = B minus A; median of d and its 95% percentile interval over targets). Ligand RMSD, stratum "all": all 17 contrasts read "not distinguishable; within 0.1 A"; no interval limit is farther than 0.007 A from 0 (for example HW(V6,G) +0.00 [-0.00, +0.00] A on 285 units of 57 targets; SD(V6) -0.00 [-0.00, +0.01] A on 570 units). Stratum "flipped" (the units whose registered top-1 differs): HW(V5,V6) -0.00 [-0.02, +0.02] A (53 units, 41 targets), HW(V6,G) -0.01 [-0.03, +0.02] A (60, 39), RC(G) +0.02 [-0.02, +0.05] A (21, 18) and HW(G,C) on 60 targets +0.01 [-0.03, +0.07] A (22, 19), each "not distinguishable; within 0.1 A". The other flipped strata have 1 to 4 units: HW(G,C) of the main study (1 unit, -0.03 A) and the float32 pairs (0 to 4 units each, every value between -0.06 and +0.06 A). The rule reads them mechanically as "B higher" (HW(V5f,V6f), HW(V6f,Gf), HW(V5f,Gf), HW(Hf,Gf)), "B lower" (HW(G,C) main, HW(Gf,Af), HW(Hf,V5f)) or "not distinguishable" (HW(Hf,Af), HW(V5f,Af), HW(V6f,Af)), all "within 0.1 A". HW(Hf,V6f) and RC(Gf) have no flipped unit with an RMSD ("not computed"). Every flipped stratum with units lies within 0.1 A; the largest limit is 0.07 A. Means (descriptive, beside the medians): the flipped strata have tails. HW(V6,G) flipped mean -0.13 [-0.44, +0.03] A: two of its 60 units are 7N4W (seeds 2 and 4, the L4 top-1 closer by 2.35 and 5.91 A), and without those two units the mean is +0.01 A (computed while writing this row, from `units.csv`). HW(V5,V6) flipped mean +0.08 [-0.09, +0.35] A, RC(G) flipped +0.10 [-0.01, +0.30] A, HW(G,C) on 60 targets flipped +0.11 [-0.08, +0.40] A, SD(V6) +0.02 [-0.11, +0.20] A (median of the absolute d 0.05 A). ipTM: the median of d is 0.000 [0.000, 0.000] in every stratum with units, and the median of the absolute d is 0. The ipTM in the output files (`*_summary_confidences.json`, which `analysis/collect.py` reads) has two decimals, so most paired differences are exactly 0 and the measure cannot resolve smaller changes. Means: HW(V5,V6) -0.001 [-0.001, -0.000] (all) and -0.002 [-0.005, -0.000] (flipped), the others with intervals containing 0. Mean pLDDT: "not distinguishable" in all 17 "all" strata (medians from -0.01 to +0.02, limits from -0.13 to +0.27) and in 8 flipped strata. The flipped strata with 1 to 6 units read "B higher" in 6 float32 pairs and "B lower" in HW(G,C) of the main study, with values between -0.41 and +0.62. One flipped stratum is "not computed". Reading: when the platform changes the returned sample, the median quality of the returned ligand pose does not change. The median paired difference of ligand RMSD from the experimental structure stays within 0.1 A in every contrast, among all predictions and among those whose top-1 changed, as it does for recompilation and for a change of seed. The means show a few large single differences, chiefly 7N4W. In each of the three changes, BCa takes its limits at the 5.9 to 7.4 and 99.4 to 99.7 percentiles of the replicates instead of 2.5 and 97.5. Both the bias correction (more replicates below the estimate than above) and the acceleration (skew of the jackknife) are positive, so the interval moves up. With 3 or 4 events, this rests on a discrete replicate distribution, the limitation item d of the specification names. Zero-event statements (F7): every replicate equals 0 (the BCa interval is degenerate, as specified); the Clopper-Pearson 95% upper limits on the fraction of targets with an event are 6.0 points for RC(V5), RC(V6) and RC(Gf) (60 targets) and 30.8 points for RC(V5f), RC(Ad) and RC(Hd) (10 pilot targets). For the follow-up 14 agree rows, the same bound is 8.9 to 20.5 points, reported and not read. Reading: a post hoc sensitivity analysis with BCa intervals on the same replicates would change 3 of 53 readings, all towards "beyond recompilation", and none away from it. | 2026-11-14 | Post hoc, descriptive count of frozen outputs, the rounded ranking score in the main study (label `rounded`); outside the pre-registered study, reported whatever it shows. (a) Question: how often does the ranking score that AlphaFold 3 writes rounded to two decimals pick a different top sample than the full-precision score AlphaFold 3 ranks by? Discussion recommendation (4) of the paper quoted "about half of the pilot's predictions" from the row of 2026-10-12 (pilot2). This row gives the main study's value. (b) Data: `results/analysis/main_20261014/primary_v2/samples.csv` (`analysis/collect.py`'s table, frozen), rows with label `main`, cache `fresh` (the first, compiling run of each process; the warm reruns `w` are left out) and exit code 0. A prediction is one process's (run, platform, target, seed, repetition) with exactly the five samples 0 to 4, each with both scores. Any other group is left out and listed in `dropped.csv`. (c) Fields: `ranking_score` is AlphaFold 3's full-precision score from `<target>_ranking_scores.csv`; `ranking_score_summary` is the same score as `*_summary_confidences.json` writes it, rounded to two decimals (row of 2026-10-12; `analysis/collect.py`). (d) Rules: the full-precision top is the sample with the highest `ranking_score`; the rounded top is the sample with the highest `ranking_score_summary`; an exact tie goes to the lowest sample index in both (as AlphaFold 3 and `contrasts.top` break ties; the M2 cross-check of 2026-10-12 found no full-precision tie at the top). A prediction "differs" when the two tops are different samples; it "has a tie" when two or more samples share the highest rounded score. Counts per platform and repetition and in total; no interval (a count of all predictions, not an estimate over targets). (e) The 2,952 predictions: every fresh process of the main study, across its eight arms and both repetitions. V5, V6, G and Gd have r1 and r2 on 60 targets with seeds 1 to 5 (600 each); GT has r1 only (300); V6h and Gh have r1 on seeds 1 and 2 (120 each); C has 6 targets with seeds 1 and 2 (12). That is 4 x 600 + 300 + 2 x 120 + 12 = 2,952, with no group left out. It is not 3,000 or a multiple of 300 because the arms differ in size. (f) Already computed, before this row: on 2026-11-14, while checking recommendation (4) for the paper, the agent ran this count once, inline (not from a script in the repository), with the definition of items b to d. The only difference is that the inline code required five samples and no missing rounded score, but did not check the sample indices or the full-precision score. Result: 1,533 of 2,952 predictions (51.9%) differ, and 74.4% have a tie at the rounded top. Per platform: V5 310 of 600, V6 310 of 600, G 313 of 600, Gd 310 of 600, GT 154 of 300, V6h 69 of 120, Gh 63 of 120, C 4 of 12. The reproduction must give the same values; a difference is reported, not corrected. (g) Post hoc status: computed after every result was read, for the wording of a recommendation. It is descriptive and changes no registered reading. (h) Code: `analysis/rounded_score.py`, tests `analysis/tests/test_rounded_score.py` (6 synthetic tests), `scripts/analysis_runs/rounded/reproduce.sh` (tests first, then the count), at commit `c2149be` (branch `paper`). Run from the main checkout: `DATE=YYYYMMDD bash scripts/analysis_runs/rounded/reproduce.sh`. Outputs in `results/analysis/rounded_DATE/`: `rounded.csv`, `dropped.csv`, `report.md`, `run.log`, `analysis_commit.txt`. | Recommendation (4) quoted a pilot value; the main study's value was counted on 2026-11-14 without a script in the repository. (a) Question: when the platform changes which sample AlphaFold 3 returns, how different are the two returned poses? The registered M4 compares samples with the same index; this analysis compares the two returned samples. (b) Data, all frozen and local: the per-sample tables and seed pairs of the main study (`results/analysis/main_20261014/primary_v2`: HW(V5,V6), HW(V6,G), RC(G)) and of follow-ups 3 and 9 as `analysis/h100.py` builds them (`h100_20261104/pairs_seed.csv`: HW(H,A), RC(H), RC(A); H from `h100_20261104/samples.csv`, A from `a100_20261018/samples.csv`), and the sample mmCIF files under `results/af3/` that `model_path` names. All 1,500 samples of each of V5, V6 and G (r1 and r2), A (r1 and r2) and H (r1 and r2) have their file locally, so no file is fetched from the bucket. (c) Units and measures. Level "seed": each registered (target, seed) unit, with each side's registered top-1 (`pairs_seed.csv`). Level "target": each target, with each side's main output, the sample with the highest full-precision ranking score across the seeds both sides ran (exact ties to the lowest seed, then sample), as AlphaFold 3 chooses it. Per unit: the ligand RMSD between the two returned samples after superposing their pockets (`score_ligand.pair_divergence`, the function of M4; 0 when the coordinates are identical as written), each sample's ligand RMSD from the experimental pose (M1) and its PoseBusters validity. Counts: distinct poses (the two returned poses more than 2 A apart); validity changes (both validities known and different); binding-mode changes (distinct, one returned pose within 2 A of the experimental pose and the other beyond 4 A; chosen here before any value, and 7N4W's flip, 1.2 against 7.1 A, meets it). A unit whose pose RMSD cannot be computed is listed and leaves the statistics that need it (the three glycan targets, whose ligand lacks atom O1). (d) Statistics per contrast, level and stratum (all units; units whose returned sample differs): the median and the 90th percentile of the pose RMSD and the shares of the three counts, each with a 95% percentile interval from the bootstrap over targets (`contrasts.boot_ci`, 10,000 replicates, a fresh generator from seed 2026 for every statistic); the maximum with its target. No correction for multiple comparisons. (e) Reading: descriptive, no rule. The platform contrasts are read beside the recompilation contrasts of the same GPUs (RC(G), RC(A), RC(H)). (f) Checks: 1, every registered top-1 names exactly one row of its per-sample table; 2, that row is the argmax of the ranking score under `contrasts.top` and has the registered success (the analysis stops if 1 or 2 fails); 3, reported, the number of targets whose main output changes, beside the earlier post hoc count of Results IV-B (16 of 60 for HW(V5,V6), 25 for HW(V6,G)). (g) Already seen before this row: every registered result and every earlier post hoc result, including M4 between samples of the same index, the flips that matter with both top-1 samples' RMSD from the experimental pose (exploratory analysis (e): 33 of 39 PoseBusters toggles, 7N4W the one binding-mode change), the `returned` analysis (RMSD from the experimental pose, top-1 of B minus top-1 of A), and the counts of changed main outputs (16 and 25). The ligand RMSD between two different returned samples has not been computed or looked at. To prepare this row, the agent read the code and the column headers. A call of the pose function from the worktree failed only because the structure and reference paths resolve against the checkout that runs the code; no value was computed. (h) Post hoc status: asked by the reviewers after the results; descriptive; it changes no registered reading and is reported whatever it shows.
