Questions. (A4) Follow-up 14 did not measure the recompilation of its float32 arms on the A100 and the H100: Table III shows "not measured" for RC(Af) and RC(Hf), and D_F of the primary pair (Hf,Af) was read with the RC(Hf) term set to 0 (item d of its row). How often does a recompilation, a new process on a new VM with the same settings, change the top-ranked sample of Af and of Hf, and is it bit-identical? (A3) The main study's L4 Triton arm (GT, configuration `l4_triton`, AlphaFold 3's GPU default) has no recompilation of its own: how often does a recompilation change GT's top-ranked sample, beside RC(G)? (a) Settings. Every arm is the r2 of an existing arm, run with that arm's configuration unchanged; no AlphaFold 3 file, no file of `af3_tpu/`, `src/`, `cloud/`, `inputs/`, `targets/`, no harness module and no entry of `harness/configs.yaml` changes. Af r2: `a100_xla_fu14_f32` on `a2-ultragpu-1g`, Hf r2: `h100_xla_fu14_f32` on `a3-highgpu-1g` (follow-up 14's float32 trunk: `GlobalConfig.bfloat16` set to `none` through `harness/fu13_probe.py`, `JAX_DEFAULT_MATMUL_PRECISION=highest`); GT r2: `l4_triton` on `g2-standard-8` (Triton flash attention, `XLA_FLAGS=--xla_gpu_enable_triton_gemm=false`, preallocation, memory fraction 0.95), bfloat16 trunk at the default precision. Every r2 keeps its r1's AlphaFold 3 v3.0.4 commit `85c4d20` with `af3_tpu/af3_tpu.patch`, weights, frozen inputs of `inputs/manifest.csv`, 10 recycles, 5 samples, one seed per process, one fresh process per (target, seed) with an empty compile cache, and the software stack recorded by r1 (jax, jaxlib, jax-cuda12-plugin and -pjrt 0.10.2; `pip_freeze.txt` byte-identical in every GT r1 session and every follow-up 14 GPU session, SHA-256 prefix `40de80f2`; NVIDIA driver 580.178.04 in every GPU session of the study; kernel 6.8.0-1070-gcp in every GT r1 and follow-up 14 GPU session). `harness/plans/config_diff_rcx.py` (run on 2026-11-14) compares every recorded setting of the r1 sessions with the r2 plans: the configuration entry, the environment, the run_alphafold command, the stack, the AlphaFold 3 commit, the patch and `run_alphafold.py` SHA-256, recycles, samples, timeout and manifest are the same; the differences are those listed here. Image: Af r2 and Hf r2 are created from the project image family `af2-tpu-benchmark/af3-accel-2204-nvidia-580` (image `af3-accel-2204-nvidia-580-v20261104a`, a copy of Canonical's `ubuntu-accelerator-2204-amd64-with-nvidia-580-v20261104a`; row of 2026-11-13), as follow-up 14's last GPU launches. The wrapper logs show that 2 of the 15 Af r1 parts (`f32_af_13` and `f32_af_15`, which hold the pilot targets 7NPL and 7U3J) ran on that image, and the other 13 Af r1 parts and all 10 Hf r1 parts on Canonical's family `ubuntu-os-accelerator-images/ubuntu-accelerator-2204-amd64-with-nvidia-580` between 2026-11-12 20:12Z and 2026-11-13 00:48Z, when the family's newest image was `...-v20261105` as first published (inferred from the family's history; Canonical deleted that image on 2026-11-13 and published an image of the same name at 15:13Z, which cannot be shown identical to the first). So for 8 of the 10 pilot targets of Af and all 10 of Hf, r2's image differs from r1's in operating-system packages, with the same driver, kernel and Python packages; this cannot be avoided, and check 4 reports it per session. GT r1 (seven sessions, VMs created on 2026-10-13 between 12:08Z and 18:05Z) ran on Canonical's family, whose newest image then was `ubuntu-accelerator-2204-amd64-with-nvidia-580-v20261009` (published 2026-10-12 18:33Z; the next, `...-v20261016`, on 2026-10-16; the image is not recorded per session, so this is inferred from the family's history). That image is still available (status READY, deprecated, read-only listing of 2026-11-14), so before the launch Lorenzo copies it into the project as image `af3-accel-2204-nvidia-580-v20261009`, family `af3-accel-2204-nvidia-580-gtr1`, as the copy of 2026-11-13 was made, and GT r2 is created from it (`IMAGE_PROJECT=af2-tpu-benchmark IMAGE_FAMILY=af3-accel-2204-nvidia-580-gtr1`, an override `cloud/af3_run.sh` accepts). Other differences of GT r2 from GT r1, none in a setting of the computation: Eigen from the archive of the same commit (`eigen_source: archive`, tag 3.4.0, commit `3147391d`, as every plan since follow-up 7; GT r1 cloned it from GitLab); Spot instead of on demand; and the CPython patch release that the VM setup installs. Found while preparing this row: the setup's `uv sync --frozen --python 3.12` takes the newest 3.12 that the uv of the day offers (uv is installed fresh on every VM), and every session up to 2026-10-26 recorded Python 3.12.15 (uv 0.13.0 to 0.13.5), every session from 2026-10-27 Python 3.12.16 (uv 0.13.6 to 0.13.8), with the same Python packages; no earlier row records this. Follow-up 13 measured that this change of setup does not change the computation on the TPU: P0 (2026-11-11, Python 3.12.16) was identical to the main study's V6 r1 (2026-10-13, Python 3.12.15) in every output file for 120 of 120 (target, seed), and L for 10 of 10 on each of v5e and v6e. The CCD is read at build time as in every earlier arm. GT r2 is therefore a recompilation of GT with the same image (inferred), driver, CUDA user space, compiler and flags, and is reported as such, with the Python patch release named. (b) Arms. Part A4 (mandatory): Af r2 and Hf r2, the 10 pilot targets, seeds 1 to 5 (50 processes each), `rep_start: 2`; their r1 are follow-up 14's Af and Hf (sessions of `scripts/analysis_runs/fu14/sessions.txt`). Part A3 (conditional, item g): GT r2, the 60 targets, seeds 1 to 5 (300 processes); its r1 is the main study's GT (`results/analysis/main_20261014/sessions_main.txt`). Part A3 is included because (1) the stack of GT r1 can be repeated as item a states, with the image available and the driver unchanged, and (2) the worst cases of item g, 36.55 USD for part A4 and 13.10 USD for part A3, together 49.65 USD, are within the cap of 62 USD; since one Spot preemption of every part would take that sum to 99.27 USD, part A3 starts only after part A4 has ended and only if the gate of item g, on part A4's actual spend, answers GO; otherwise part A3 is not run and is reported as not run. No other arm. Out-of-memory stops and other failures are recorded with their cause, never hidden or rerun with other settings; a (target, seed) missing on either side of a contrast leaves it (section 8) and the counts are reported. (c) Checks, before any result is read: (1) every process exits 0 or its failure is recorded; (2) one machine type per condition, the r1's (`a2-ultragpu-1g`, `a3-highgpu-1g`, `g2-standard-8`); (3) completeness per condition and seed; (4) settings and stack: `af3_entry_stats.json` records `global_config_bfloat16: none` and `xla_env` `JAX_DEFAULT_MATMUL_PRECISION=highest` in every Af r2 and Hf r2 process and neither in any GT r2 process; every process names the expected device (NVIDIA A100-SXM4-80GB, NVIDIA H100 80GB HBM3, NVIDIA L4); for every r2 session against the r1 sessions of its arm, the NVIDIA driver (`device_check.txt`), the kernel and Python version (`session.json`), `pip_freeze.txt` byte for byte and the packages of `setup.json` (AlphaFold 3's build-date suffix removed) are compared and any difference is listed (for GT the Python patch release of item a is expected); a difference other than those of item a is reported beside the arm's result and does not stop the analysis; (5) bit-identity, item e 1. The analysis stops if check 1 or 2 fails. (d) Statistics: as section 5, the target-clustered bootstrap with 10,000 replicates and seed 2026, every contrast paired on (target, seed) with the per-seed top-1 (M2). RC(Af) = Af r1 against Af r2 and RC(Hf) = Hf r1 against Hf r2 on the 10 pilot targets, seeds 1 to 5 (50 (target, seed) each); RC(GT) = GT r1 against GT r2 on the 60 targets, seeds 1 to 5 (300): the top-1 flip rate, the success flip rate and flips that matter, the M4 medians and 90th percentiles (ligand and global C-alpha RMSD between the two sides' samples), the M5 statistics and the fraction of bit-identical samples (`contrasts.summarize`). As for RC(V5f) and RC(Gf) in follow-up 14, these are rates with their intervals and a bit-identity count; no threshold. Expected precision: on 50 (target, seed) from 10 targets, one flip gives 2.0% and an interval of about [0, 6]; RC(A) and RC(H) on the same targets and seeds were 8.0% [2.0, 14.0] and 2.0% [0.0, 6.0] (follow-up 14). (e) Reading rules, fixed now, none beyond those of follow-up 14 for RC(V5f), RC(Gf), RC(Ad) and RC(Hd). (1) For each of Af, Hf and GT: "recompilation bit-identical" if every (target, seed) of r2 is identical to r1; "not bit-identical" with the number of (target, seed) that differ otherwise; "not determined" if files are missing on a side and none differs. For Af and Hf every output file is compared (`contrasts.outputs_identical`, the mmCIF timestamp line masked: the strict check of follow-up 14 item b2), after read-only copies from the results bucket of the full outputs of Af r1 and Hf r1 on the pilot targets (`scripts/analysis_runs/rcx/fetch_full_outputs.py`; r2 is fetched in full). For GT, which r1 fetched light, the files of the light fetch are compared (the top model and the five sample mmCIFs, the ranking scores and the summary confidences, mmCIF timestamp masked), because the full files of 600 processes, about 27 GB, do not fit on the Mac (29 GiB free on 2026-11-14). RC(Af), RC(Hf) and RC(GT) are reported with their intervals, beside the registered RC(A) and RC(H) on the pilot targets, RC(Ad), RC(Hd), RC(V5f) and RC(Gf) (follow-up 14's `contrasts.csv`, copied); they fill the RC cells of Table III that read "not measured". (2) D_F sensitivity, descriptive: D_F of the primary pair (Hf,Af) = [HW(Hf,Af) minus RC(Hf)] minus [HW(H,A) minus RC(H)] with RC(Hf) measured instead of 0, (i) on the 60 targets of the registered D_F, with the same 10,000 draws of targets (seed 2026) and RC(Hf)'s rate taken over the pilot targets among each replicate's draws (0 in a replicate that draws none of them, a probability of about 2 in 100,000 per replicate; their number is reported), and (ii) on the 10 pilot targets alone, every term on them, beside the same with RC(Hf) set to 0. The registered D_F (60 targets, RC(Hf) = 0; -10.3 [-15.3, -5.3] top-1, "reduced") is first recomputed and must equal follow-up 14's `d_f.csv`. The registered reading stands whatever (i) and (ii) show; the report states whether (i) would read the same under rule e1 of follow-up 14. If RC(Hf) has no flip, (i) equals the registered D_F by construction. (3) RC(GT) against RC(G), descriptive: RC(GT) minus RC(G) on the 60 targets and seeds 1 to 5 for the three flip statistics, with the target bootstrap (`fu13.diff_two`), RC(G) from the main study's `pairs_seed.csv` (7.3% top-1); no reading rule. (f) Provisioning and launch, for the shortest time to the last result; not a change in any computation. Quotas read on 2026-11-14 (read-only): GPUS_ALL_REGIONS 10; A100 80 GB on demand and Spot us-central1 2, us-east4 1, us-east5 1, europe-west4 1; L4 on demand and Spot 16 in every region read; Spot H100 64 per region (Cloud Quotas, row of 2026-11-12); no VM of the project running. All three arms on Spot; a preempted part is relaunched unchanged. us-east4 is left out on every GPU (the setup failures of 2026-11-12 there): the A100 wrapper with `A100_REGION_CAPS="us-central1:2 us-east5:1 europe-west4:1"` and `MAX_A100=4`, the H100 parts with `ZONES` us-central1-a, -b, -c and us-east5-a and `MAX_H100=3`, the L4 parts with the launcher's L4 zones less us-east4-a and -c and `MAX_L4=8`. Every wrapper's `LAUNCHER` is `scripts/monitor/rcx_guarded_launch.sh` (item g). The A100 and H100 wrappers start together (7 GPUs); the L4 wrapper starts after part A4 (8 GPUs). Parts hold whole targets, as many as the platform's slots (Af 4, Hf 3, GT 8), each at most 4 h of job at the worst factor, targets dealt longest first; deadline ceil(worst job min x 1.25 + 20). `FETCH=full` for Af r2 and Hf r2; GT r2 fetched light, as GT r1. The supervisor of follow-up 14, copied for this follow-up (`~/Desktop/af3_drafts/rcx_supervisor.sh` with `rcx_sup_state.py`, outside the repository; their SHA-256 is recorded in the launch row), relaunches unchanged the parts whose last session failed for a transient reason (no VM, Spot preemption or VM gone, setup failure before any AlphaFold 3 run; at most 4 rounds per platform, none once the stop marker exists) and applies the gate of part A3 when part A4 has ended. The provisioning model, the zone and the image enter no contrast. The launches run under `caffeinate -dims` from the main checkout at the commit that merges the implementation. (g) Cost and budget. From `cloud/prices.csv` and the median process wall time per bucket of the r1 processes of the same configuration (Af 119.3, 193.3, 322.5 and 508.7 s, Hf 76.2, 111.5, 172.5 and 264.2 s for buckets 256 to 1,024, the values of follow-up 14's `fu14_20261113b/cost.csv`; GT 115.1, 176.8, 267.6 and 393.6 s, main study) and their per-part overheads (provisioning, setup and teardown: Af 0.4, 8.4 and 1.2 min; Hf 0.7, 7.3 and 1.3; GT 6.8, 12.3 and 1.3), times 1.0 expected and 1.25 worst (the largest ratio of a session's process time to these medians is 1.01 for Af, 1.01 for Hf and 1.02 for GT). Prices, the highest Spot row among the regions each wrapper uses: A100 3.02 USD/h (on demand 5.54), H100 6.54 USD/h (on demand 12.37), L4 0.54 USD/h (on demand 0.90). Part A4: Af r2 4 parts, 4.48 VM h, 13.53 USD expected, 16.41 worst; Hf r2 3 parts, 2.55 VM h, 16.73 USD, 20.14 worst; together 30.26 USD expected, 36.55 worst, 73.10 if every part were preempted once at the end of its worst job and relaunched. Part A3: GT r2 8 parts, 19.99 VM h, 10.75 USD expected, 13.10 worst, 26.17 with one preemption per part. Follow-up 14's Spot losses (sessions that did not complete) were 12% of the complete sessions' spend on the A100 and 3% on the H100. Budget, on the Google TPU Builders credit only: 87 USD read by Lorenzo on 2026-11-14 (a further grant of 1,000 USD has been requested and is not counted). Cap of follow-up 15: 62 USD (the balance minus the floor of 25 USD). Budget rule before the A100 and H100 wrappers (`scripts/monitor/credit_check_rcx.sh a100|h100 BALANCE`, `scripts/monitor/rcx_budget.py`): GO if the balance, minus what the other part A4 wrapper may still spend when it is running (its worst case less its spend), minus this wrapper's worst case, stays at or above 25 USD, and the spend so far plus those stays within 62 USD. Gate of part A3 (`credit_check_rcx.sh l4 BALANCE`, applied by the supervisor with the balance estimated as 87 USD minus follow-up 15's spend from the session records at list prices, as follow-up 14's item b2 gate): GO only if every part A4 part has a complete session and no follow-up 15 session is running, the balance minus part A3's worst case stays at or above 25 USD, and follow-up 15's spend so far plus part A3's worst case stays within 62 USD; so part A3 runs if part A4 has spent at most 48.90 USD. Stop rule, before every part of every wrapper (`scripts/monitor/rcx_guarded_launch.sh`, which runs `rcx_budget.py guard` and then `cloud/af3_run.sh` unchanged): the projected spend is follow-up 15's spend so far from the session records (VM running to deletion at the list price of its provisioning) plus what every other part still needs (its expected cost less what a running session of it has spent; parts of the guarded part's platform not yet started at 0, parts of part A3 only once part A3 has started) plus this part's expected cost; if it exceeds 62 USD, the part is not launched (no VM is created), the wrapper starts no further part, the reason is written to `results/rcx_cap_stop.txt`, every later part is refused while that file exists, and Lorenzo decides in a row; running parts finish. A part of part A3 is also refused while a part A4 part has no complete session. Every gate also refuses a launch after 2026-11-17 or an expected end after 2026-11-18. Download to the Mac: the full outputs of Af r2 and Hf r2 and the read-only copies of Af r1 and Hf r1, about 9 GB (about 45 MB per process, follow-up 14's full sessions), and GT r2 light, about 1 GB, against 29 GiB free. (h) Duration and calendar. From the first VM to the last result, at the expected factor and without capacity waits: A100 1.1 h (1.4 worst), H100 0.9 h (1.1), then the L4 2.6 h (3.2); with the session check, the copies and the analysis, about one day from the launch. Launch aim 2026-11-15, so that the analysis is done on 2026-11-16, before the 2026-11-17 aim and the paper version of 2026-11-20; hard limits: launch by 2026-11-17, every run finished by 2026-11-18 (`launch_by` and `finish_by` in every plan), else the arm is reported as incomplete; analysis by 2026-11-19. The implementation is committed and the OSF addendum registered before the first run. No run is dropped or selected on results; an interrupted part is relaunched unchanged; every launch, relaunch, gate and stop is recorded in section 10. (i) Already seen before this row: every registered result of the main study and follow-ups 1 to 14, among them GT's own contrasts (SD(GT), HW(V5,GT), HW(V6,GT), HW(GT,C), DU), RC(G) 7.3% top-1, follow-up 14's ten float32 pairs, D_F of (Hf,Af) with RC(Hf) set to 0 (-10.3 [-15.3, -5.3]), RC(Gf) 0 flips (with 0.3% of samples bit-identical), RC(V5f) 10 of 10 identical, RC(Ad) and RC(Hd) bit-identical (50 of 50 each), RC(A) and RC(H) on the pilot targets, DET(A), DET(H) and the cost tables; and the post hoc analyses of 2026-11-14: `returned` (17 contrasts, among them the ten float32 pairs, RC(G) and RC(Gf)), `rare` (BCa intervals, including the zero-event statements RC(V5f), RC(Ad), RC(Hd) and RC(Gf), and the Clopper-Pearson bound of 30.8 points on 10 targets) and `rounded` (GT: the rounded score picks another top sample in 154 of 300 predictions). Not seen: no recompilation of Af, Hf or GT exists. The `warm_th` fresh processes of GT (2026-11-11, 10 pilot targets, seed 1, `l4_triton`) are a later run of GT's configuration; they were never compared with GT r1 and are not used here (their row: they enter no contrast). D_F of (Hf,Af) on the pilot targets alone has not been computed. To prepare this row the agent read run records only (process wall times, per-part overheads, the image of each session in its wrapper log, `setup.json`, `device_check.txt`, `pip_freeze.txt`, the host and Python fields of `session.json`), Canonical's image listing (read-only) and the column headers of the registered tables; no output of a prediction. Also read before this row was committed: the post hoc analyses `winners` and `cpu_matched` of 2026-11-14 (main-study arms, follow-up 3 and 9 GPU arms and follow-up 4; none uses Af r2, Hf r2 or GT r2). (j) Code: `harness/plans/make_rcx_plans.py` (plans `harness/plans/rcx/`, `rcx_parts.csv`, `rcx_table.md`, parts files `results/parts_rcx_a100.txt`, `parts_rcx_h100.txt`, `parts_rcx_l4.txt`), `harness/plans/config_diff_rcx.py`, `scripts/monitor/rcx_budget.py`, `credit_check_rcx.sh`, `rcx_guarded_launch.sh`, `analysis/rcx.py` with `analysis/tests/test_rcx.py`, `scripts/analysis_runs/rcx/` (`reproduce.sh`, `check_sessions.py`, `fetch_full_outputs.py`, `sessions.txt`); recorded with their SHA-256 in the implementation row. | Table III has no recompilation for the float32 A100 and H100 arms or for the L4 Triton arm, and follow-up 14's D_F of (Hf,Af) rests on RC(Hf) = 0 |

No session mixes the two. The jax, jaxlib, libtpu and CUDA plugin versions of each arm are unchanged (supplement, Arms). On GPUs, recompilation alone changes results, so no such identity check exists there. GPU contrasts whose two sides fall in different periods also include the patch release, which the TPU check suggests is inert but does not show for GPUs: follow-up 6's recompilation on the L4 with official inputs (Go r1 on 2026-10-26 with 3.12.15, Go r2 on 2026-10-29 with 3.12.16), the H100 of follow-up 9 against the A100 of follow-up 3, the seeds 3 to 5 of follow-up 12 against the main study's L4, and the float32 arms of follow-ups 13 and 14 against their bfloat16 references from October. Session lists: the dated folders of `results/af3/` with a `session.json`; a session without one (smoke tests, failed starts, featurization-only follow-up 5 and the sessions that never started a process) records no Python version. | 2026-11-16 | Post hoc analysis of frozen outputs, returned poses compared directly for three further contrasts (label `winners_ext`), specified after the main-study and follow-up 1 to 15 outcomes and the post hoc analyses `returned`, `rare`, `rounded`, `winners` and `cpu_matched` were read, and before any value of this analysis was computed; descriptive whatever it shows, outside the pre-registered study. (a) Question: as in `winners` (row of 2026-11-14), when the platform changes which sample AlphaFold 3 returns, how different are the two returned poses? Here for the contrasts `winners` does not cover, which leave the "returned" cells of the paper's Table II empty: the L4 against the CPU, and default attention on the L4. (b) Data, all frozen and local: HW(G,C) of the main study (6 targets, seeds 1 and 2) and DU (60 targets, seeds 1 to 5) from `results/analysis/main_20261014/primary_v2` (`samples.csv`, `contrasts/pairs_seed.csv`); HW(G,C) of follow-up 4 (60 targets, seeds 1 and 2; C of the 6 main-study targets from the main study and of the other 54 from `results/analysis/cpu60_20261018/samples.csv`, with `cpu60_20261018/pairs_seed.csv`, as `returned` paired them); and, as the second recompilation reference of DU beside RC(G) of `winners`, RC(GT) of follow-up 15 (GT r1 of the main study against GT r2 of `results/analysis/rcx_20261115/samples.csv`, with `rcx_20261115/pairs_seed.csv`; 60 targets, seeds 1 to 5). Sides as registered: HW(G,C) is G r1 against C r1; DU is G r1 (side A) against GT r1 (side B), as `contrasts.build_pairs` names it; RC(GT) is r1 against r2. The sample mmCIF files under `results/af3/` that `model_path` names are all local (24, 600, 240 and 600 files for the four contrasts), so no file is fetched from the bucket. (c) Units, measures and counts: those of `winners`, unchanged and computed by its functions (`analysis/winners.py` is imported, not modified). Level "seed": each registered (target, seed) unit with each side's registered top-1. Level "target": each target with each side's main output, the sample with the highest full-precision ranking score across the seeds both sides ran (seeds 1 and 2 for HW(G,C), 1 to 5 for DU and RC(GT); exact ties to the lowest seed, then sample). Per unit: the ligand RMSD between the two returned samples after superposing their pockets (`score_ligand.pair_divergence`, the function of M4; 0 when the coordinates are identical as written), each sample's ligand RMSD from the experimental pose (M1) and its PoseBusters validity. Counts: distinct poses (more than 2 A apart); validity changes; binding-mode changes (distinct, one returned pose within 2 A of the experimental pose and the other beyond 4 A, the definition of `winners`). A unit whose pose RMSD cannot be computed (the three glycan targets) is listed in `errors.csv` and leaves the statistics that need it. (d) Statistics per source, contrast, level and stratum (all units; units whose returned sample differs): the median and the 90th percentile of the pose RMSD and the shares of the three counts, each with a 95% percentile interval from the bootstrap over targets (`contrasts.boot_ci`, 10,000 replicates, a fresh generator from seed 2026 for every statistic); the maximum with its target. HW(G,C) of the main study and of follow-up 4 are kept apart (source `main` and `cpu60`). No correction for multiple comparisons. (e) Reading: descriptive, no rule. HW(G,C) is read beside RC(G) of `winners` (the CPU has no recompilation arm), and DU beside RC(G) of `winners` and RC(GT) here. (f) Checks: 1, every registered top-1 names exactly one row of its per-sample table; 2, that row is the argmax of the ranking score under `contrasts.top` and has the registered success (the analysis stops if 1 or 2 fails); 3, reported, the number of targets whose main output changes, per source and contrast (no earlier count exists for these contrasts). (g) Already seen before this row: every registered result and every earlier post hoc result, including for these contrasts the registered flip rates and M4 between samples of the same index (Table II: DU ligand RMSD median 0.0066 A, HW(G,C) 0.0231 A), the flips that matter of DU with both top-1 samples' RMSD from the experimental pose (exploratory analysis (e)), the `returned` analysis of HW(G,C) in the main study and in follow-up 4 (RMSD from the experimental pose of both top-1 samples, B minus A), the `cpu_matched` analysis, and follow-up 15's RC(GT) flip rates; and every result of `winners` (the six contrasts it covers). The ligand RMSD between the two returned samples has not been computed or looked at for HW(G,C), DU or RC(GT), nor the main output across seeds of these contrasts. To prepare this row, the agent read the code and the column headers. A structure check printed only per-contrast unit counts (HW(G,C) main 12 seed and 6 target units; DU 300 and 60; HW(G,C) follow-up 4 120 and 60; RC(GT) 300 and 60), the number of mmCIF files each needs and that all are local, and the pass of checks 1 and 2; no pose RMSD was computed. (h) Post hoc status: asked to complete the reviewers' request answered by `winners`, after its results; descriptive; it changes no registered reading and is reported whatever it shows. (i) Code: `analysis/winners_ext.py` (it imports `analysis/winners.py` and changes nothing in it), tests `analysis/tests/test_winners_ext.py` (5 synthetic tests; the seed pairs built by the registered `followup34.build`, a synthetic pose RMSD in place of the structure files), `scripts/analysis_runs/winners_ext/reproduce.sh` (the tests of `winners_ext` and `winners` first, then the analysis with 8 processes), at commit `d777fdb`. | 2026-11-16 | Post hoc analysis of frozen outputs, which success criterion changes in a success flip and by how much (label `pb_flips`), specified after the main-study and follow-up 1 to 15 outcomes and the post hoc analyses `returned`, `rare`, `rounded`, `winners` and `cpu_matched` were read, and before any value of this analysis was computed; descriptive whatever it shows, outside the pre-registered study. (a) Question: are success flips changes of pose, or crossings of a threshold near its limit? For every success flip (the registered top-1 of one side succeeds and the other's does not), which criterion of M1 fails on the failing side (the 2 A RMSD threshold, a PoseBusters check and which, or both), and how far the failing value lies beyond its threshold or tolerance. (b) Data, all frozen and local: the per-sample tables and seed pairs of the main study (`results/analysis/main_20261014/primary_v2`: HW(V5,V6), HW(V6,G), RC(G)) and of follow-ups 3 and 9 as `analysis/winners.py` loads them (`h100_20261104/pairs_seed.csv`: HW(H,A), RC(H), RC(A); H from `h100_20261104/samples.csv`, A from `a100_20261018/samples.csv`); the saved M1 results of the two top-1 samples (`m1_ligand_rmsd`, `m1_pb_valid`, `m1_pb_failed`, `m1_success`, `m1_error`); and the top-1 mmCIF files of both sides of every success flip under `results/af3/` (41 flips: 11, 10, 5, 6, 5 and 4; 73 distinct files, all local). Groups: "platform" (HW(V5,V6), HW(V6,G), HW(H,A)) and "recompilation" (RC(G), RC(H), RC(A)). (c) Measures. From the saved results (no rerun needed): the failing side and the criterion that fails on it, "RMSD" (ligand RMSD of 2 A or more, PoseBusters-valid), "PoseBusters" (below 2 A, not valid), "RMSD and PoseBusters", or "not scored" (an M1 error); the PoseBusters checks that fail. Success needs both criteria, so nothing fails on the succeeding side. From a rerun on the 73 files only: M1 is recomputed by the registered function `score_ligand.score` (posebusters 0.6.5, configuration "dock", the predicted protein in the superposed frame as `mol_cond`; the analysis stops if posebusters is another version), then the same steps once more with PoseBusters' full report (`bust(full_report=True)`), which gives the continuous value behind each of the 16 checks that have one (bond lengths, bond angles and internal clash as the largest relative deviation from the distance-geometry bound; the two ring planarity values and the double-bond planarity in A; the energy ratio; the smallest ligand-protein distance in A; the smallest distance over the sum of van der Waals radii for protein, organic cofactors, inorganic cofactors and waters; the four volume overlaps), against its threshold from `dock.yml` (0.25, 0.25, 0.3, 0.25, 0.05, 0.25, 100, 5 A, 0.75 and 0.075). The six other checks (loading, sanitization, InChI, connectivity, radicals) are binary and have no distance. Excess over the tolerance, as a fraction of the threshold: (value minus threshold) over threshold for an upper limit, (threshold minus value) over threshold for a lower limit, above 0 fails; for the RMSD, (RMSD minus 2 A) over 2 A, 0 or above fails. Per flip: the excess of each failing criterion on the failing side and the worst (largest) of them; the excess of the same criteria on the succeeding side (how far inside); and the ligand RMSD between the two top-1 samples (`winners.pose_rmsd_real`, the pose function of M4 and `winners`). Classification, chosen here before any value: "pose change" if the two top-1 poses are more than 2 A apart (the "distinct" of `winners`), whatever the excess; otherwise "near-threshold crossing" if every criterion that fails on the failing side exceeds its tolerance by at most 10% of the threshold (for the RMSD, below 2.2 A); otherwise "other"; "not classified" if a needed value is missing (a failing binary check, a pose RMSD that cannot be computed, a check without a value). Strata: all success flips, and beside it the flips whose top-1 sample changed (9 of the 41 keep the same sample index and change its coordinates). (d) Statistics per contrast and group: counts per criterion, per failing check and per class; the shares of "near-threshold crossing" and of "pose change" among the classified flips, and the median worst excess of the failing side, each with a 95% percentile interval from the bootstrap over targets (`contrasts.boot_ci`, 10,000 replicates, a fresh generator from seed 2026 for every statistic; in a group, the flips of a target in several contrasts are pooled under that target). The per-flip table is reported in full. No correction for multiple comparisons. (e) Reading: descriptive; the classification is the only rule, and the platform group is read beside the recompilation group. (f) Checks: 1 and 2 as in `winners` (every registered top-1 names one row of its per-sample table, is the argmax of the ranking score under `contrasts.top` and has the registered success); 3, the rerun reproduces the saved M1 exactly on every one of the 73 files (the ligand RMSD as written, to 4 decimals, the validity, the set of failed checks, the success and the error); 3b, the full report gives the same failed checks as the standard report; 4, the sign of every computed excess agrees with the outcome of its check on every file. The analysis stops if any of 0 (version), 1, 2, 3, 3b or 4 fails, before any statistic is computed, and `reproduction.csv` lists the files. (g) Already seen before this row: every registered result and every earlier post hoc result, including the success-flip rates and counts of these contrasts and, for the 20 of the 26 success flips of HW(V5,V6), HW(V6,G) and RC(G) that are also flips that matter, both top-1 samples' RMSD from the experimental pose, validity and failed checks, and which criterion separates them (exploratory analysis (e); supplement tables of the flips that matter: 33 of the 39 flips that matter of its nine contrasts separated by PoseBusters validity). Not seen: the failed checks of the 6 success flips of these contrasts whose top-1 did not change and of the 15 success flips of HW(H,A), RC(H) and RC(A) (no earlier analysis tabulates them, to the agent's knowledge), any continuous PoseBusters value of a study output, and the pose RMSD between the two top-1 samples of a success flip as such (`winners_20261114/units_seed.csv` holds the pose RMSD of every seed unit of these six contrasts, the 41 flips among them; the agent did not read that file, and the summaries of `winners` do not separate the success flips). To prepare this row, the agent read the code, PoseBusters' configuration and module code, and the column headers. A mechanical check of the rerun ran `score_ligand.score` and the full report on four crystal structures as their own prediction (7U3J, 7N4W, 8G6P, 7TOM; reference data, not study outputs): about 1 s per file, the full report agreeing with the standard one. A structure check printed only the success-flip counts per contrast (11, 10, 5, 6, 5, 4), how many keep their top-1 (2, 2, 2, 1, 1, 1), the 73 files and that all are local, and the pass of checks 1 and 2; no study file was rescored. (h) Post hoc status: asked by the reviewers after the results; descriptive; it changes no registered reading and is reported whatever it shows. (i) Code: `analysis/pb_flips.py` (it imports `analysis/winners.py` and `analysis/score_ligand.py` and changes neither), tests `analysis/tests/test_pb_flips.py` (7 synthetic tests: the thresholds against the installed `dock.yml`, the excess, the criterion, the classes and the summary, and the reproduction, sign and top-1 checks stopping the analysis; the seed pairs built by the registered `followup34.build`, synthetic rescoring and pose functions in place of the structure files), `scripts/analysis_runs/pb_flips/reproduce.sh` (package versions, the tests, then the analysis with 8 processes), at commit `d777fdb`. Run from the main checkout: `DATE=YYYYMMDD bash scripts/analysis_runs/pb_flips/reproduce.sh`. Outputs in `results/analysis/pb_flips_DATE/`: `flips.csv`, `rescored.csv`, `reproduction.csv`, `summary.csv`, `checks.csv`, `inputs.tsv`, `report.md`, `run.log`, `analysis_commit.txt`. | The reviewers asked which PoseBusters checks drive the success flips and how close the failing values lie to their thresholds, to tell threshold crossings from changes of pose. |
| 2026-11-16 | Post hoc analysis of frozen outputs, success of the best of all samples per platform (label `best25`), specified after the main-study and follow-up 1 to 15 outcomes and the post hoc analyses `returned`, `rare`, `rounded`, `winners` and `cpu_matched` were read, and before any value of this analysis was computed; descriptive whatever it shows, outside the pre-registered study. (a) Question: do the differences between platforms change what a user finds by looking at all samples, or only which sample is returned? Per platform, the success of an oracle that picks a correct sample whenever one exists, beside the success of AlphaFold 3's main output, and their differences between platforms against recompilation. (b) Data, all frozen and local, per-sample tables only (no structure file is read): V5, V6 and G, r1 and r2, from `results/analysis/main_20261014/primary_v2/samples.csv`; C r1 of the 6 main-study targets from the same file and of the other 54 from `results/analysis/cpu60_20261018/samples.csv`; A r1 and r2 from `results/analysis/a100_20261018/samples.csv`; H r1 and r2 from `results/analysis/h100_20261104/samples.csv`; for checks 2 and 3, the registered `pairs_seed.csv` of the main study, follow-up 4 and the H100 analysis and the `q3.csv` of the main study and the A100 analysis. Contrasts, B minus A, A the first-named side: platform, HW(V5,V6), HW(V6,G) and HW(H,A) on seeds 1 to 5, and HW(G,C) on seeds 1 and 2 (60 targets, the pairing of follow-up 4); recompilation, the reference, RC(V5), RC(V6), RC(G), RC(H) and RC(A) on seeds 1 to 5, and RC(G) on seeds 1 and 2 (the reference of HW(G,C) on the same seeds). Sides as registered: HW(P,Q) is P r1 against Q r1, RC(P) is r1 against r2. (c) Measures, from the registered M1 success of each sample (`m1_success`; a sample without it, as on the three glycan targets, is no success, as registered, so those targets never differ). Level "target", over the seeds S of the contrast: "oracle", at least one sample of the seeds S succeeds (25 samples on seeds 1 to 5, 10 on seeds 1 and 2); "main", AlphaFold 3's main output succeeds (the sample with the highest full-precision ranking score over S, exact ties to the lowest seed, then sample; the rule of `winners`). Level "seed", each (target, seed) of S: "oracle", at least one of the seed's 5 samples succeeds; "top-1", the seed's registered top-1 succeeds. (d) Statistics: per side and seed set, the rate of each measure (`rates.csv`); per contrast, level and measure, the difference of the rates (B minus A), the units where exactly one side succeeds (A only, B only, and the number of targets with such a unit) and their share ("discordant"), each rate, difference and share with a 95% percentile interval from the bootstrap over targets (`contrasts.boot_ci`, 10,000 replicates, a fresh generator from seed 2026 for every statistic; units of a drawn target pooled). No correction for multiple comparisons. (e) Reading: descriptive, no rule. Each oracle row is read beside the main-output row (target level) or the top-1 row (seed level) of the same contrast, and each platform contrast beside recompilation (HW(V6,G) and HW(H,A) beside RC(G), RC(H) and RC(A); HW(G,C) beside RC(G) on seeds 1 and 2; HW(V5,V6) beside RC(V5) and RC(V6)). (f) Checks: 1, every (side, target, seed) of a contrast has exactly 5 samples with a ranking score, and both sides hold the same targets with every seed of S; 2, the top-1 sample and its success recomputed for every registered seed unit equal `pairs_seed.csv` (1,500 units of the main study, 120 of follow-up 4, 900 of the H100 analysis; RC(G) on seeds 1 and 2 is a subset); the analysis stops if 1 or 2 fails; 3, reported, the main-output success rate on seeds 1 to 5 equals the registered Q3 success rate with 5 seeds for V5, V6 and G (main study) and A (A100 analysis); H and C have no registered Q3 rate with 5 seeds. (g) Already seen before this row: every registered result and every earlier post hoc result, including the per-seed top-1 success of every side (`pairs_seed.csv`), the Q3 success rates of the top-ranked sample over 1 to 5 seeds and their differences (supplement tables Q3), the seed-consensus success rates of the `ranking` analysis, and follow-up 14's success table. To the agent's knowledge no earlier analysis computed whether any sample of a seed or of a target succeeds; that has not been computed or looked at. To prepare this row, the agent read the code and the column headers. The analysis completed (it stops before any statistic otherwise). Results. Criterion: of the 41 success flips (on 17 targets), 2 fail on the RMSD alone and 39 on PoseBusters alone, none on both and none unscored; in all 39, the one check that fails on the failing side is `minimum_distance_to_protein` ("Minimum distance to protein" of `dock.yml`: it fails when a ligand heavy atom and a protein heavy atom are closer than 0.75 times the sum of their van der Waals radii, the protein being the predicted one in the superposed frame); the 39 fall on 17 targets. The two RMSD flips are one event: the v6e's top-1 of 7N4W seed 4 (sample 3, 7.12 A from the experimental pose) against the v5e's (sample 1, 1.20 A) in HW(V5,V6) and the L4's (sample 1, 1.21 A) in HW(V6,G), 6.67 A apart in both, the binding-mode change of `winners`. The one target-level oracle difference of HW(V5,V6) and of HW(V6,G) is the same event: 7XBV, where a sample of the v6e on seed 1 succeeds and none of the 25 of the v5e or the L4 does; that of HW(G,C) is 7MGT, where a sample of the L4 on seed 1 succeeds and none of the CPU's 10. Read beside (descriptive, no rule, as the specification fixes): at the target level, the oracle differs between the two platforms of a pair on at most 1 of 60 targets (0 for HW(H,A)) and under recompilation on none, while the main output differs on 3, 4, 1 and 1 targets (HW(V5,V6), HW(V6,G), HW(H,A), HW(G,C)) and under recompilation on 0 to 3. | 2026-11-16 | Follow-up experiment 16, the HLO of AlphaFold 3's model as XLA compiles it on the v5e and on the v6e (label `hlo`), specified after the main-study and follow-up 1 to 15 outcomes and the post hoc analyses listed in item i were read, and before any run; reported as a follow-up, outside the pre-registered study, whatever it shows. Question. The HPC reviewer (R1) of the blind reviews of 2026-11-14 and 2026-11-15 asks where the compiler generates different code on the two TPU generations. Follow-up 13's layer capture (L) found the v5e and the v6e already different at the Pairformer's input (depth 0 of recycle 0, after the input embedding, the templates and the MSA module), from inputs identical across platforms (follow-up 5); AlphaFold 3 issue #772 locates an A100 case in an early bfloat16 step (a template normalisation). Here the program itself is read: the HLO module of the model (`jit_apply_fn`) after XLA's optimisations on each chip, compared in each of the study's four token buckets and located per AlphaFold 3 module. Pre-specified question: do the differences between the two chips' HLO appear already in the operations that precede the Pairformer? (a) Settings. Each arm is the main study's arm of its chip (V5: `tpu_xla` on `v5litepod-1`; V6: `tpu_xla` on `ct6e-standard-1t`) with one change: the configuration `tpu_xla_hlo` of `harness/configs.yaml`, which is `tpu_xla` plus the variable `AF3_HLO_DUMP_FLAGS=--xla_dump_hlo_as_text --xla_dump_hlo_module_re=jit_apply_fn`. That variable is read only by `harness/af3_entry.py`, in a block added for this follow-up (additive; without the variable nothing in it runs and `XLA_FLAGS` is untouched, so every earlier configuration runs as before): before AlphaFold 3 and JAX are imported, it sets `XLA_FLAGS` to `--xla_dump_to=<process folder>/xla_dump` followed by the variable's flags (`tpu_xla` sets no `XLA_FLAGS`), because XLA reads `XLA_FLAGS` once, when the backend starts, and because each process needs its own dump folder: every AlphaFold 3 process of a session runs in the same working directory and XLA names dump files by a module number that repeats from process to process, so a fixed `--xla_dump_to` in the configuration would let the processes overwrite each other's dumps. XLA then writes, per process, the model's module as text before (`module_*.jit_apply_fn.before_optimizations.txt`) and after its optimisations (`module_*.jit_apply_fn.*after_optimizations.txt`, with companion files such as the buffer assignment), each instruction with its `op_name` (the Haiku module path, for example `diffuser/evoformer/__layer_stack_no_per_layer/trunk_pairformer/...`) and a stack frame resolved by the tables at the head of the file (source file, function and line). What is set is recorded per process in `af3_entry_stats.json` (`hlo_dump`: the flags, the number and size of the files, the optimised module's file name). Why the dump does not change the compilation: XLA's dump options are debug options that write files and set no compiler pass; JAX leaves them out of its persistent-cache key because they "do not affect the compile result" (`jax/_src/cache_key.py`, jax 0.10.2, `xla_flags_to_exclude_from_cache_key` and `_hash_serialized_compile_options`); the harness gives every process a fresh, empty compile cache, so every process compiles. Measured on the Mac before this row (jax and jaxlib 0.10.2 of the AlphaFold 3 environment, CPU backend; `analysis/tests/test_hlo.py`): a jit-compiled function run through `harness/af3_entry.py` with and without the variable gives byte-identical results, and the dump lands in the process folder. On the TPU, compilation runs in libtpu, which receives XLA's debug options with the compile options; that libtpu honours them is expected and only the run can show it, so check 2 below makes the run's own outputs the test: a run whose outputs are not bit-identical with the registered ones is invalid. Everything else is the main study's: AlphaFold 3 v3.0.4 commit `85c4d20` with `af3_tpu/af3_tpu.patch`, the weights, the frozen inputs of `inputs/manifest.csv`, 10 recycles, 5 samples, XLA attention, one fresh process per (target, seed) with an empty compile cache, jax and jaxlib 0.10.2 with libtpu 0.0.43.2 (build `libtpu_lts_20260630_b_RC06`), the v5e TPU runtime `v2-alpha-tpuv5-lite` and the v6e image family `ubuntu-os-accelerator-images/ubuntu-accel-2204-amd64-tpu-v5e-v5p-v6e` (the launcher's defaults). Differences from the main study's sessions, none in a setting of the computation: the label; the Eigen source of AlphaFold 3's build (the archive of the same commit, `3147391d`, plan key `eigen_source: archive`, as every plan since follow-up 7); the CPython patch release the VM setup installs (3.12.15 in the main study, 3.12.16 since 2026-10-27; row of 2026-11-14: follow-up 13's P0 and L were identical with the main study's V6 and V5 r1 across that change); and the newest image of the v6e family at creation, which the session does not record (follow-up 13's P0 and L of 2026-11-10 and 2026-11-11 were identical with the main study's r1 on images of that date). `harness/plans/config_diff_hlo.py` (run on 2026-11-16) compares every recorded setting of the main study's V5 and V6 sessions with the two plans: platform, provisioning, weights, machine type, image or runtime, libtpu and its build, configuration entry apart from the variable, environment (`xla_env`), stack, AlphaFold 3 commit, patch and `run_alphafold.py` SHA-256, buckets, recycles, samples, timeout and manifest are the same; the differences are the label, the configuration's name, description and variable (and so the run_alphafold command, which shows it), the repetitions and seeds, and the Eigen source; 0 settings differ beyond these. (b) Arms. V5dump: `tpu_xla_hlo` on the v5e; V6dump: `tpu_xla_hlo` on the v6e; one part each (`harness/plans/hlo/hlo_v5_01.yaml`, `hlo_v6_01.yaml`), one VM each. Targets: one per token bucket of the study, the pilot target with the most tokens in that bucket (the least padding): 7U3J (205 tokens, bucket 256), 7NPL (431, bucket 512), 7XQZ (539, bucket 768) and 7D5C (1,023, bucket 1,024); `make_hlo_plans.py` checks the rule against `inputs/manifest.csv`. AlphaFold 3 pads every input to shapes set by its bucket (tokens and atoms) and by fixed sizes (MSA rows, templates, chains; `features.PaddingShapes` in `model/pipeline/pipeline.py`), so the compiled program depends on the bucket, not on the target, and one target per bucket gives that bucket's program. Seed 1 only: the seed enters the program as a run-time key, not as a constant, so the program does not depend on it. Two fresh processes per (target, seed), r1 and r2, on the same VM (`fresh_reps: 2`), in the harness's order (r1 of the four targets by tokens, then r2): r2 is a second compilation in a new process, which shows whether the dumped HLO of a chip is the same from compilation to compilation (rule e4; expected yes, since recompilation on the TPU has been bit-identical in every arm measured) and gives check 2 a second process per target. 16 processes in all (8 per chip). No other arm. (c) Checks (`analysis/hlo.py`), before any HLO is compared: (1) every process exits 0 (`run.json`) and its `xla_dump/` holds exactly one optimised and one unoptimised text dump of `jit_apply_fn`; (2) validity: every process's outputs are identical bit for bit with the registered process of the same chip, target and seed, the main study's r1, seed 1 (V5: session `20261013T132954Z_v5e_main_v5_01`, V6: `20261013T205257Z_v6e_main_v6_01`, run `tpu_xla_rep1_seed1_fresh`, whose full outputs, `*_data.json` and every `*_confidences.json` included, are on the Mac for all four targets: 21 files per process, counted on 2026-11-16), every output file compared with the mmCIF timestamp line masked (`contrasts.outputs_identical`); (3) the device each process names (`TPU v5 lite`, `TPU v6 lite`); (4) the libtpu build `libtpu_lts_20260630_b_RC06`, jax, jaxlib 0.10.2 and libtpu 0.0.43.2 (`session.json`), the dump flags each process ran with (`af3_entry_stats.json`), and the Python version, listed. Checks 3 and 4 are reported beside the result and do not stop it; checks 1 and 2 decide rule e1. (d) Measures, descriptive, no statistics (one program per chip and bucket). The units of a module are its instructions in every computation that runs (the entry, loop bodies and conditions, called and fused computations; the reducer computations of reductions enter their caller's signature instead). Each unit has: a stage, the AlphaFold 3 module it belongs to, from its `op_name` and its stack frames: `embedding` (the input embedding: `evoformer_conditioning` and the evoformer's pair and single embeddings, relative encoding, bonds, recycling embeddings), `template` (`template_embedding`, `Evoformer._embed_template_pair`), `msa` (`msa_stack`, `msa_activations`, `extra_msa_target_feat`, `Evoformer._embed_process_msa`), `pairformer` (`trunk_pairformer`, the trunk's 48 blocks), `diffusion` (`diffusion_head`, the files `diffusion_head.py` and `diffusion_transformer.py`, `Model._sample_diffusion`), `confidence` (`confidence_head`), `distogram` (`distogram_head`), `params` (reading a weight, `op_name` `params[...]`), `other` (model glue with metadata) and, for a unit without metadata, the stage of its fusion, else of its first operand that has one (`unattributed` if none); a semantic signature for arithmetic (opcode, element types and dimensions of result and operands, and every attribute that sets the computation: precision, algorithm, contracting, batch and reduced dimensions, windows, comparison, custom-call target, the reducer's operations and types; never names, layouts, metadata or backend configuration), none for operations that move data exactly (bitcast, copy, reshape, transpose, broadcast, slices, concatenate, pad, reverse, iota, gather) and for control (parameters, tuples, fusion, loop and call instructions); a types signature (the semantic one without dimensions); the semantic signatures of the innermost fusion it sits in, with the fusion kind; a layout signature (opcode, result shape with layout and tiling, and the non-semantic attributes, for every unit except control); and its backend configuration, if any. Comparison of two modules, per stage, on the multisets of units: the level is the first that differs among semantic (which arithmetic operations run, with which types, shapes, precisions and reductions; marked "types" if the types signatures differ too, else "shapes"), fusion (the same operations grouped into other kernels), layout (the same kernels with other layouts or data movement), backend (the same HLO with another backend configuration), else identical. Tables, per bucket, chip and stage: counts of units per opcode, fusion kind and element type; the dot and convolution precisions and operand types; the reductions (opcode, operand and result types, reducer, dimensions); and the module paths (the `op_name`'s Haiku modules below the stage, to depth 4) whose units differ between the chips at each level, with the count on each side. (e) Reading rules, fixed now. (1) Validity: the run is valid if checks 1 and 2 pass for all 16 processes; otherwise every table is still written, marked invalid, and not read, and the run is reported as invalid; a run with outputs that differ from the registered ones is not repeated with other settings without a new row. (2) Level of each (bucket, stage), v5e against v6e, r1 of each chip, as item d. (3) The pre-specified question, per bucket: "yes" if the highest level among `embedding`, `template` and `msa` is semantic or fusion; "layout only" if it is layout; "backend only" if it is backend; "no" if all three are identical; overall, the answer of the four buckets if they all agree, else "mixed" with each bucket's answer. (4) Within-chip determinism, per chip and bucket: r1 against r2 "identical" if the optimised dumps are byte-identical, else the level per stage; a cross-chip level of a stage at or below the within-chip level of that stage on either chip is reported as "not attributable to the chip". (5) The unoptimised dumps of the two chips, per bucket: "identical" if byte-identical below the module's first line, else the level per stage (expected identical: JAX lowers the same program for both TPU generations, so differences would come from XLA). Interpretation stated now: the optimised HLO is the last form of the program that XLA dumps as text; the TPU backend's code generation below it (tiling, the order of accumulation inside a kernel, the use of the matrix units) is visible only through the backend configuration. So "yes" places the source of follow-up 13's measured difference before the Pairformer in XLA's HLO passes, at the modules and operations the tables name (other arithmetic, or the same arithmetic grouped into other kernels); "layout only" says that XLA ran the same operations in the same kernels with other layouts or data movement, which are exact, so the numerical difference comes from the code the backend generates for those layouts; "backend only" or "no" place it below the HLO, in code generation. No direction is predicted. (f) Provisioning and launch, not a change in any computation. Both arms on demand (`v5litepod-1` at 1.56 USD/h in europe-west4-b, `ct6e-standard-1t` at 2.97 USD/h in europe-west4-a, `cloud/prices.csv`): each run lasts about an hour, and a Spot preemption would cost a relaunch, more than Spot saves (about 1.3 USD in all). Quotas read on 2026-11-16 (read-only): the legacy TPU API's `TPUV5sLitepodPerProjectPerZoneForTPUAPI` 512 (the default row, which covers europe-west4-b); Compute Engine `TPUS-PER-TPU-FAMILY-per-project-region` for CT6E 48 in europe-west4 and 1 in us-east5 (approved on 2026-11-12, row of that day); no Compute Engine instance and no TPU VM in europe-west4-a or -b. One wrapper (`cloud/af3_run_many.sh` through `~/Desktop/af3_drafts/retry_arm_par.sh`, parts file `results/parts_hlo.txt`, `MAX_V5E=1`, `MAX_V6E=1`, `FETCH=full` on both lines), whose `LAUNCHER` is `scripts/monitor/hlo_guarded_launch.sh` (item g). v5e in europe-west4-b (`SPOT=0`); the reserve `results/parts_hlo_v5e_ew4a.txt` (europe-west4-a) only after a row. v6e with `PROVISIONING=standard` and `ZONES=europe-west4-a,us-east5-b,us-east5-a,us-east5-c`, tried in that order (europe-west4-b left out: INTERNAL_ERROR for this machine type on 2026-11-12); the on-demand list price of the v6e in us-east5 is 2.70 USD per chip-hour (SKU `C4E3-1652-BF17`, "TpuV6e running in Columbus", service Compute Engine `6F81-5844-456A`, Cloud Billing Catalog API `https://cloudbilling.googleapis.com/v1/services/6F81-5844-456A/skus`, read-only on 2026-11-16; the same listing gives 2.97 for europe-west4, SKU `5682-23F1-7895`, and 1.56 for the v5e in europe-west4, SKU `6F35-2B43-18B4`, as `cloud/prices.csv`); if the VM is created in us-east5, the completion row states the spend at 2.70 USD/h, while the budget rules of item g keep 2.97, the higher price. A part that fails for a transient reason (no capacity, setup failure before any AlphaFold 3 run) is relaunched unchanged. The zone and the provisioning enter no comparison. The launch runs under `caffeinate -dims` from the main checkout at the commit that merges the implementation. (g) Cost and budget. From `cloud/prices.csv` and the main study's median process wall per bucket (V5: 70.9, 103.6, 163.2 and 261.6 s at 256, 512, 768 and 1,024 tokens; V6: 70.5, 96.5, 139.2 and 205.6 s) and per-part overheads (provisioning, setup, teardown: V5 2.6, 12.4 and 1.7 min; V6 3.8, 9.4 and 1.4 min), times 1.1 expected and 2.0 worst (writing the dump; its size on the TPU is not known): V5dump 0.64 VM h, 1.01 USD expected, 1.47 worst; V6dump 0.56 VM h, 1.65 USD expected, 2.41 worst; together 2.66 USD expected, 3.88 worst; deadline ceil(worst job min x 1.25 + 20): 86 and 75 min; the lifetime bound (deadline + 30 min, v5e rounded up to whole hours) 3.12 + 5.20 = 8.32 USD, the most both parts can cost before their launchers delete the VMs. Budget, on the Google TPU Builders credit only: 40.47 USD read by Lorenzo on 2026-11-16; floor 25 USD; cap of follow-up 16: 15 USD (the balance less the floor is 15.47 USD; the lifetime bound of 8.32 USD and one full relaunch of both parts at their worst case, 3.88 USD, fit within it). Rule before the wrapper (`scripts/monitor/credit_check_hlo.sh BALANCE`, `scripts/monitor/hlo_budget.py check`): GO if the balance minus the lifetime bound of every part without a complete session stays at or above 25 USD, the spend so far plus that bound stays within 15 USD, the launch is on or before 2026-11-17 and the worst end by 2026-11-18 (UTC). Stop rule before every part (`scripts/monitor/hlo_guarded_launch.sh`, which runs `hlo_budget.py guard` and then `cloud/af3_run.sh` unchanged): the projected spend, follow-up 16's spend so far from the session records (VM running to deletion at the on-demand price) plus what the other part still needs (its worst case less what a running session of it has spent; 0 once it has a complete session) plus this part's worst case, must stay within 15 USD and the launch on or before 2026-11-17; otherwise no VM is created, the reason goes to `results/hlo_cap_stop.txt`, every later part is refused while that file exists, and Lorenzo decides in a row. Dump size: not measured on the TPU before the run. The optimised module holds each layer stack's body once (the 48 Pairformer blocks are one loop body), so its text is expected in the tens of MB per process, with companion files of similar size; at most a few GB for the 16 processes, against 100 GB of boot disk per VM and 53 GiB free on the Mac (2026-11-16). No compression on the VM: `analysis/hlo.py` also reads `.txt.gz`, so the dumps may be compressed on the Mac after the analysis. (h) Duration and calendar. From the first VM to the last result, both parts at once and without capacity waits: 0.64 h expected, 0.94 h worst; with the session check and the analysis, a few hours from the launch. Launch aim 2026-11-16 or 2026-11-17, so that the analysis is done by 2026-11-18 and enters the paper version of 2026-11-20; hard limits: launch by 2026-11-17, every run finished by 2026-11-18 (`launch_by` and `finish_by` in both plans), else the arm is reported as not run. The implementation is committed and the OSF addendum registered before the first run. No run is dropped or selected on results; an interrupted part is relaunched unchanged; every launch, relaunch, gate and stop is recorded in section 10. (i) Already seen before this row: every registered result of the main study and follow-ups 1 to 15, among them follow-up 13's L (recycle 0, depth 0, median over the 10 pilot targets: V5 against V6 single 0.0041 u, pair 1.08 u; the first depth at which every pair of platforms exceeds the L4's recompilation level is 0 for every target) together with its P and F arms, follow-up 14's float32 arms and follow-up 15's recompilations; the post hoc analyses `returned`, `rare`, `rounded`, `winners` and `cpu_matched` (2026-11-14) and `winners_ext`, `pb_flips` and `best25` (specified and analysed on 2026-11-16, commits `ae4c38a` and `c06ea97`; their results were not read to prepare this row, and none of them reads a compiled program); AlphaFold 3 issue #772 (A100-PCIE-40GB, 12 of 4,194,304 bfloat16 values of the first affected template normalisation; read 2026-11-14); the two blind reviews. Not seen: no HLO of AlphaFold 3 has been dumped or read in this study. To prepare this row the agent read run records only (process wall times and overheads of the main study's V5 and V6 sessions, `session.json`, the device and compile fields of `run.json` of 7U3J and 7D5C, launcher events, the number of reference output files of the four targets), the Cloud Billing Catalog listing of TPU prices, the TPU quotas (read-only) and the source of AlphaFold 3 (the Haiku module names), JAX (`cache_key.py`) and the harness; the dumps it read were of toy functions and a small Haiku model on the Mac's CPU, not of AlphaFold 3. (a) Checks (item c), all pass: (1) 16 of 16 processes exit 0, each with exactly one optimised and one unoptimised dump of `jit_apply_fn`; (2) 16 of 16 processes identical in every output file (mmCIF timestamp line masked) with the registered process of the same chip, target and seed, the main study's r1 seed 1 (`20261013T132954Z_v5e_main_v5_01` and `20261013T205257Z_v6e_main_v6_01`, run `tpu_xla_rep1_seed1_fresh`), so the dump left the computation unchanged on both chips; (3) 16 of 16 name the expected device (`TPU v5 lite`, `TPU v6 lite`); (4) 16 of 16 with libtpu build `libtpu_lts_20260630_b_RC06`, jax and jaxlib 0.10.2, libtpu 0.0.43.2 and the dump flags of the configuration; Python 3.12.16 in every process. (b) Results, read with the rules of item e. Rule e1: valid. Rule e2: in each of the four buckets, every stage (`embedding`, `template`, `msa`, `pairformer`, `diffusion`, `confidence`, `distogram`, `params`, `other`, `unattributed`) differs between the chips at level semantic, detail "types". Rule e3: "yes" in bucket 256, 512, 768 and 1,024; overall "yes": the two chips' HLO already differs in the operations that precede the Pairformer. Rule e4: on each chip and in each bucket, r1 and r2 wrote byte-identical optimised dumps (8 of 8), so no cross-chip level is "not attributable to the chip". Rule e5: the unoptimised dumps of the two chips are byte-identical below the module's first line in all four buckets. (c) Descriptive, as item d's tables give them: `module_diffs.csv` lists 2,667 (bucket, level, stage, module path) rows with differing units (370 semantic, 627 fusion, 921 layout, 749 backend). The optimised HLO of both chips expresses every matrix product as a `convolution`; in `dots.csv` the products of `embedding`, `template` and `msa` have the same counts per operand types, result type and precision on both chips in every bucket, and 28 of 116 rows differ, all in `other`, `diffusion` and, in bucket 512, `pairformer`. The units per stage of the tables, and so the stage columns of every descriptive table, are affected by the defect of item f. (d) Descriptive, before the Pairformer, from the corrected attribution of item f (the code of the row that follows this one, run on the same 8 r1 dumps outside the registered output; the rerun of that row replaces these numbers): the number of matrix products is the same on both chips in every bucket (embedding 34, template 31, msa 40), with the same precision attributes. In `template` and `msa` no operation type (opcode, element types, precision, reducer; scalar constants left aside) exists on one chip and not on the other: the semantic differences there are different counts of the same operations. In `embedding` the operand or result types of three products differ: `evoformer_conditioning_pair_mlp_1` (v5e bfloat16 activations and bfloat16 result; v6e float32 activations and bfloat16 result, the only type of operation present on one chip only, v6e), `evoformer_conditioning_project_atom_features_for_aggr` (v5e float32 activations, v6e bfloat16) and the output projection of the atom transformer's gated linear unit (v5e float32 activations, v6e bfloat16 after an explicit conversion); other embedding products differ only in the order of their dimensions. Conversions in bucket 256 (bfloat16 from float32, float32 from bfloat16), v5e against v6e: embedding 13 and 15 against 12 and 13, msa 52 and 89 against 50 and 88, template 42 and 79 on both; they sit in different modules on the two chips (v5e: the recycling projection `prev_embedding`, a bfloat16 round trip in `evoformer_conditioning_pair_mlp_2`, the gating projections of both triangle multiplications of the MSA stack; v6e: `position_activations`, the output projections of the triangle multiplications). Float32 elementwise operations: v6e has 2 more in embedding, 7 in template, 13 in msa; no bfloat16 elementwise operation and no reduction differs in count before the Pairformer. In the MSA stack's `pair_attention1` and `pair_attention2` (op_name `tokamax ... DotProductAttention`, which on both chips is lowered to XLA operations, not to a kernel of its own), the logits' scale, bias and mask (multiply, add, select on float32[128,4,256,256] in bucket 256) are computed in the output fusion of the query-key product on both chips, and computed again in the two softmax fusions on the v6e only (6 more units per attention). The `PRNGSequence.reserve` units are Haiku's derivation of random keys (threefry: 32-bit unsigned integer add, xor, shifts and or, and data movement, no floating-point operation); both chips hold the same set of these operations and the v5e more copies of them (bucket 256: 3,926 against 3,587 units, of which 684 against 575 in `template`). `other` holds, after the correction, 1,682 to 1,914 units per chip and bucket (model-level key derivation and operations whose op_name names no module); `unattributed` 3,016 to 5,436 units without metadata, mostly data movement the TPU compiler inserts (tuple elements, asynchronous copies, slices), of which 244 to 308 arithmetic or constant in bucket 256. Scalar constants differ in count in every stage (v6e fewer). (e) The detail "types" of rule e2 is not an artefact of order or names (the comparison is on multisets; names, metadata and layouts are left out), but in `template` and `msa` it arises from counts only: the same operation types appear a different number of times on the two chips (for example the attention logits computed once on the v5e and three times on the v6e, and the threefry operations above). (f) Defect of the implementation found after the outputs were read, while preparing item d; no rule changes. The stage of a unit (item d) uses its `op_name` and its stack frames, and `analysis/hlo.py` reads both incompletely: (1) XLA's text dump prints each frame's `parent_frame_id` as the parent's id plus one (a root frame prints 1); `Module.stack` followed it unchanged and stopped at frames whose printed parent equals their own id, so for most units it saw only the innermost frame (in the v5e 256 dump, 8,225 of 14,091 frames print themselves as parent); read with one subtracted, the chain is the Python call stack of the operation (checked on the real TPU dumps and on XLA's CPU dump of the test model, where the frames of a matrix product run `Linear.__call__`, `Blk.__call__`, `Evo.__call__`, `Diffuser.__call__`); (2) op_name components wrapped by a transformation, such as `vmap(confidence_head)`, were not recognised as module names, and components that AlphaFold 3 builds from the prefix `evoformer_conditioning` (for example `evoformer_conditioning_atom_transformer_encoder`) did not match `evoformer_conditioning`; (3) with complete stacks, the file rule for `diffusion_transformer.py` also catches the Pairformer's single attention, which calls `diffusion_transformer.self_attention`, so the module names of the op_name must decide before the stack frames. Effect, measured outside the registered output on the 8 r1 dumps: units per stage change (bucket 256, v5e: diffusion 7,917 to 19,655, other 17,105 to 1,901, confidence 448 to 2,435, msa 1,370 to 3,167, embedding 3,329 to 2,686, template 3,247 to 3,506, pairformer 1,192 to 1,258); every stage of every bucket is still at level semantic, detail types; rule e3 is still "yes" in all four buckets; rules e1, e4 and e5 do not depend on stages. The registered readings of item b therefore stand; the stage columns of the descriptive tables of `hlo_20261116` are not read, and the correction and a rerun are recorded in the next row. | Follow-up 16 analysis, as specified; a stage-attribution defect found and its effect measured |

| 2026-11-21 | Post hoc analysis of frozen outputs, the L4 against the A100 with AlphaFold 3's default Triton attention on both (label `gtat`), specified on 2026-11-21 after the main-study and follow-up 1 to 16 outcomes and every earlier post hoc analysis (`returned`, `rare`, `rounded`, `winners`, `cpu_matched`, `winners_ext`, `pb_flips`, `best25`) were read, and before any value of this pair was computed; outside the pre-registered study, reported whatever it shows. (a) Question: the GPU pairs of the study use XLA attention, while AlphaFold 3's default on a GPU is its Triton attention kernel. With that default on both GPUs, does the L4 return other predictions than the A100, beyond what recompilation already changes? Two arms already hold this configuration on the same 60 targets and seeds, GT of the main study and AT of follow-up 3, and their pair HW(GT,AT) has never been computed. (b) Data, all frozen and local, per-sample tables and registered seed pairs only (no structure file is read, no session is collected): GT r1 (60 targets, seeds 1 to 5, the 7 main-study GT sessions) from `results/analysis/main_20261014/primary_v2/samples.csv`; AT r1 (60 targets, seeds 1 to 5, the 3 AT sessions of `scripts/analysis_runs/a100/sessions.txt`) from `results/analysis/a100_20261018/samples.csv`; RC(GT) of follow-up 15 from `results/analysis/rcx_20261115/pairs_seed.csv`; HW(G,A) of follow-up 3 from `results/analysis/a100_20261018/pairs_seed.csv`; every session in the freeze `4ec185c5` (`results/freeze/followups1316_20261117/sessions.tsv`). Sides: GT is side A (the first-named platform, as G in HW(G,A)), AT side B. (c) Seed pairs and statistics: every (target, seed) both sides ran (300 expected), each side's top-1 the sample with the highest ranking score among the seed's 5 (`contrasts.top`, the registered M2); the three flip statistics of section 5, top-1 flip, success flip and flips that matter, as `contrasts.pair_rows` defines them; each rate with a 95% percentile interval from the paired bootstrap over targets (`contrasts.boot_ci`, 10,000 replicates, random-number seed 2026, a fresh generator for every statistic). M4, M5 and the bit-identical fraction are not computed (M4 and the bit-identical fraction need the structure files; the question is at decision level). Glycans as registered (row of 2026-10-14): the samples of 7OZC, 7WDT and 7WJB have no M1 and count as no success on the 60 targets; every statistic is also reported on the other 57 targets. (d) Recompilation reference: HW(GT,AT) minus RC(GT), with `contrasts.hw_minus_rc` unchanged (RC(GT) as RC(P), the first-named platform, as interpretation 5 of 2026-10-09 fixes; the same resampled targets for both terms), RC(GT) as follow-up 15 measured it (7.7% [4.7, 11.0] top-1 on 60 targets; its r2 ran Python 3.12.16 against 3.12.15, as declared there). RC(AT) was never measured, so the secondary reference RC(Q) does not exist for this pair, and the reading below names RC(GT) only. (e) Descriptive comparison: HW(GT,AT) minus HW(G,A), the same two GPUs with XLA attention (follow-up 3), on the same resampled targets (the bootstrap of `contrasts.hw_minus_rc`, applied to the two HW seed-pair sets); the registered rows of HW(G,A), its HW minus RC(G) and minus RC(A), RC(A) and RC(GT) are copied beside. (f) Reading rule, fixed now, the registered rule for HW minus RC: for each flip statistic on the 60 targets, "beyond RC(GT)" if the 95% interval of HW(GT,AT) minus RC(GT) lies above 0, otherwise "not beyond RC(GT)"; the rows on 57 targets are read with the same rule and reported beside. Against HW(G,A), wording only, no decision: "larger with Triton" if the interval of the difference lies above 0, "smaller with Triton" if below 0, otherwise "no difference shown". No correction for multiple comparisons. (g) What the paper reports, whatever the outcome: the HW(GT,AT) top-1 and success flip rates and the three HW minus RC(GT) values with intervals, beside HW(G,A); labelled post hoc, specified on 2026-11-21 after every result was read, with RC(AT) not measured; it changes no registered reading. Outcome 1, top-1 beyond RC(GT), success flip and flips that matter not beyond: the default configuration changes which sample the two GPUs return beyond recompilation on the L4, but not measurably whether it succeeds, the pattern of the registered GPU pairs. Outcome 2, none beyond: with the default attention the two GPUs differ no more than a recompilation of the L4 Triton arm, read with the caveat that recompilation on the A100 Triton arm is unknown. Outcome 3, the success flip or flips that matter beyond: a decision-level difference under the default configuration, reported with its events and targets. In every outcome the full table (rates on 60 and 57 targets, the three readings, the comparison with HW(G,A)) goes to the supplement. A pre-registered follow-up with Triton attention on the H100, planned and not yet specified, is the confirmatory test; this analysis does not replace it. (h) Checks, before any statistic; 1 to 4 stop the analysis if they fail: 1, sessions: the AT rows come from exactly the 3 AT sessions of `scripts/analysis_runs/a100/sessions.txt`, the GT rows from main-study GT sessions, all in the freeze; one machine type per side (`g2-standard-8`, `a2-ultragpu-1g`), attention `triton` on every row, the expected device (NVIDIA L4, NVIDIA A100-SXM4-80GB), label and repetition r1, every process exit 0; 2, completeness: both sides hold the same 60 targets, each with seeds 1 to 5 and exactly 5 scored samples; 3, registered top-1: for every seed unit, the recomputed top-1 and its success equal side B of DU (main study) and side A of RC(GT) (follow-up 15) for GT, and side B of DU(A) (follow-up 3) for AT; 4, method: from their registered seed pairs, the same code reproduces the registered flip rows of HW(G,A) and RC(GT) and the six registered rows of HW(G,A) minus RC(G) and minus RC(A) within 1e-9; 5, reported: the targets whose every sample on both sides lacks M1 (atom O1 missing) are exactly 7OZC, 7WDT and 7WJB. (i) Already seen before this row: every registered result and every earlier post hoc result, including for these two arms DU (G against GT) and DU(A) (A against AT) with M4 and M5; HW(G,A) (top-1 12.0% [8.3, 16.0]), HW(G,A) minus RC(G) (+4.7 [0.3, 9.3] points) and minus RC(A) (+2.0 [-1.7, 5.7]); RC(A) (10.0% [6.7, 13.3]); RC(GT) of follow-up 15 and RC(GT) minus RC(G); `winners_ext` for DU and RC(GT). Each side's per-seed top-1 and success are in the registered seed pairs (DU, DU(A), RC(GT)), never paired with each other. The Q3 rows of follow-up 3 (`results/analysis/a100_20261018/q3.csv`, the supplement's Q3 tables of follow-up 3) hold the success rate of the main output of GT and of AT over 1 to 5 seeds and the difference of those two rates: a difference of success rates, not a paired flip rate; the agent did not open those rows for this row. No earlier output pairs GT with AT per (target, seed): every contrast name in `results/analysis/` was listed, and none does. To prepare this row, the agent read the code and the column headers, listed the sessions, machine types, devices, attention and row counts of the two sides, and ran checks 1 to 5 of the code on the real inputs, which printed only pass or fail and counts (all pass: 7 GT and 3 AT sessions in the freeze, 300 processes exit 0 per side, 60 targets complete, 300 seed units equal to each registered table, the registered rows reproduced, the three glycan targets as expected; a first version of check 5 looked for samples without an M1 value and found none, because the registered tables store these samples as no success with an M1 error, so the check now reads the M1 error); no HW(GT,AT) seed pair was built and no value of it was computed. (j) Post hoc status: asked by the reviews of 16 November, which note that the GPU arms of the primary pairs use XLA attention rather than AlphaFold 3's default; specified after every result was read, without this pair ever computed; descriptive in standing, read with the registered HW minus RC rule; it changes no registered reading and is reported whatever it shows. (k) Code: `analysis/gtat.py` (it imports `contrasts` and `followup34` and changes nothing in them; SHA-256 `ee90db63bc0e468955c0ac5fd37d0b4269f1299c12491b8db26dd45043a39bc6`), tests `analysis/tests/test_gtat.py` (9 synthetic tests; the registered tables built by `followup34.build`, `contrasts.summarize` and `contrasts.hw_minus_rc`; SHA-256 `9461a6fad367cde01601b3f00a7da1c7c23905f683ae17cb516b77b784865e35`), `scripts/analysis_runs/gtat/reproduce.sh` (tests first, then the analysis; SHA-256 `d39d0dea05cbe55c7a271fedb2ad160fa519425ae706605eb716bf1d7b8da070`), committed with this row in branch `gtat` (worktree `~/Desktop/af3-gtat`, from `main` at `24fb242`). Outputs in `results/analysis/gtat_DATE/`: `pairs_seed.csv`, `rates.csv`, `hw_minus_rc.csv`, `versus_hw_g_a.csv`, `registered.csv`, `checks.csv`, `inputs.tsv`, `report.md`, `run.log`, `analysis_commit.txt`. | The GPU pairs use XLA attention, not AlphaFold 3's GPU default; the two Triton arms already ran on the same targets and seeds, and their pair was never computed. |
