| 2026-11-24 | Follow-up experiment 17, the float32 trunk at the default matmul precision (label `dtp`), specified after the main-study and follow-up 1 to 16 outcomes and the post hoc analyses up to 2026-11-21 were read (item i) and before any run; reported as a follow-up, outside the pre-registered study, whatever it shows. Questions. (T) Follow-ups 13 and 14 changed two settings at once: AlphaFold 3's `GlobalConfig.bfloat16` from `all` to `none` (the data type of the trunk's activations) and `JAX_DEFAULT_MATMUL_PRECISION` from the backend's default to `highest` (the precision of float32 matmuls without an explicit precision). With both, TPU v6e and the L4 rank the same sample first in 299 of 300 (target, seed) predictions (HW(V6f,Gf) 0.3% [0.0, 1.0]); with the precision alone, in bfloat16, they do not (HWh(V6,G) 20.7% [16.0, 25.7], follow-up 12, five seeds). The fourth cell of this two-by-two design, float32 activations at the default precision, was never run. With the trunk's activations in float32 and the float32 matmuls at each backend's default, does the v6e and L4 disagreement fall, and does it fall to agreement? At that default the trunk's matmul operands are rounded to single-pass bfloat16 on the TPU (8 significand bits) and to TensorFloat-32 on the L4 (11 bits), with float32 activations, accumulation and elementwise arithmetic on both, so the cell also measures how much of the TPU and GPU difference the two operand formats leave once the activations are float32. (G, part B) Does the answer hold on the A100 80 GB and the H100 80 GB, and what does the float32 trunk cost there at the default precision, where follow-up 14's highest precision cost 1.43 and 1.47 times the bfloat16 wall time (2.20 and 1.89 at 1,024 tokens)? (a) Settings. The new configurations are the float32-trunk configurations of follow-ups 13 and 14 less the line `JAX_DEFAULT_MATMUL_PRECISION: highest`, that is the main-study configurations plus `AF3_FU13_BFLOAT16: none` (read by `harness/fu13_probe.py` through `harness/af3_entry.py`, as in follow-ups 13 and 14); entries appended to `harness/configs.yaml`, none changed: `tpu_xla_dtp_f32def` (`tpu_xla` plus it), `l4_xla_dtp_f32def` (`l4_xla` plus it), `a100_xla_dtp_f32def` (`a100_xla` plus it), `h100_xla_dtp_f32def` (`h100_xla` plus it). No AlphaFold 3 file, no file of `af3_tpu/`, `src/`, `cloud/`, `inputs/`, `targets/` and no harness module changes. Explicit-precision sites (among them the grid attention of the `xla` arm) are unchanged, as in every arm. Every arm keeps the settings of its source arm: AlphaFold 3 v3.0.4 with `af3_tpu/af3_tpu.patch`, the same weights, software stacks (jax 0.10.2; libtpu 0.0.43.2 on TPU; jax-cuda12-plugin and -pjrt 0.10.2 on GPU), frozen inputs of `inputs/manifest.csv`, 10 recycles, 5 samples, machine types (`ct6e-standard-1t`, `g2-standard-8`, `a2-ultragpu-1g`, `a3-highgpu-1g`), XLA attention, the XLA flags and memory settings of `l4_xla`, `a100_xla` and `h100_xla`, the Eigen archive, one fresh process per (target, seed, repetition) with an empty compile cache. GPU image: the project family `af2-tpu-benchmark/af3-accel-2204-nvidia-580` (image `af3-accel-2204-nvidia-580-v20261104a`, NVIDIA driver 580.178.04), as follow-up 15's A100 and H100 arms; TPU image family as in follow-up 13. The machine setup installs the newest CPython 3.12 patch release of the day (3.12.16 in every session since 2026-10-27, row of 2026-11-14); check 4 records it. (b) Arms. Part A (mandatory): V6fd (TPU v6e, `tpu_xla_dtp_f32def`), the 60 targets, seeds 1 to 5, r1, and r2 of the 10 pilot targets, seed 1 (check 5); Gfd (L4, `l4_xla_dtp_f32def`), the 60 targets, seeds 1 to 5, r1 and r2 in the same part (one VM, as Gf of follow-up 13 and the main study's G). Part B (conditional, item g): Afd (A100, `a100_xla_dtp_f32def`) and Hfd (H100, `h100_xla_dtp_f32def`), the 60 targets, seeds 1 to 5, r1. The other three cells come from earlier rows, used as they are: V6 and G r1 and r2 (main study), V6h and Gh on five seeds (main study and follow-up 12), V6f and Gf r1 and r2 (follow-up 13); for part B, A and Ah (follow-up 3), H (follow-up 9), Af and Hf (follow-up 14), RC(A) and RC(H) as registered. Out-of-memory stops and other failures are recorded with their cause, never hidden or rerun with other settings; a (target, seed) missing on either side of a contrast leaves it (section 8) and the counts are reported. (c) Checks, before any result is read: (1) every process exits 0 or its failure is recorded; (2) one machine type per condition; (3) completeness per condition, repetition and seed; (4) the settings reached every process: `af3_entry_stats.json` records `global_config_bfloat16: none` in every V6fd, Gfd, Afd and Hfd process, `session.json`'s `xla_env` holds no `JAX_DEFAULT_MATMUL_PRECISION` in any of them, every process names the expected device (TPU v6 lite, NVIDIA L4, NVIDIA A100-SXM4-80GB, NVIDIA H100 80GB HBM3), and the driver, kernel, Python version and `pip_freeze.txt` of every session are compared with those of the source arm's sessions, any difference listed; (5) V6fd r2 against V6fd r1, the 10 pilot targets, seed 1, every output file byte for byte (`contrasts.outputs_identical`, the mmCIF timestamp line masked), reported; a difference is reported and RC(V6fd) on the 60 targets is then not assumed to be 0 anywhere; (6) memory: the compiled device memory per bucket of every new condition against the device limit, every out-of-memory stop listed (follow-up 13's float32 trunk at the highest precision used at most 52% of a device's limit; the default precision does not add memory). The analysis stops if check 1 or 2 fails. (d) Statistics: as section 5, the target-clustered bootstrap with 10,000 replicates and seed 2026, every contrast paired on (target, seed) with the per-seed top-1 (M2), seeds 1 to 5, all terms of a difference on the same resampled targets. The four cells of the pair (V6,G), each a top-1 flip rate between v6e and the L4: c_bd = HW(V6,G) (bfloat16, default; main study), c_bh = HWh(V6,G) (bfloat16, highest; follow-up 12), c_fd = HW(V6fd,Gfd) (float32, default; new), c_fh = HW(V6f,Gf) (float32, highest; follow-up 13). Primary: c_fd, and D_T = [HW(V6fd,Gfd) minus RC(Gfd)] minus [HW(V6,G) minus RC(G)], built as follow-up 13's D_F with Gfd in place of Gf (RC(Gfd) measured, item b). Secondary, the decomposition, each with its interval: the data-type effect at the default precision T_d = c_fd minus c_bd; the precision effect in float32 P_f = c_fh minus c_fd; the interaction I = (c_fh minus c_fd) minus (c_bh minus c_bd); the success flip rate and flips that matter of the four cells; RC(Gfd) beside RC(G) (7.3%) and RC(Gf) (0.0%), and RC(Gfd) minus RC(G); F_d(V6) = V6 against V6fd and F_d(G) = G against Gfd, and V6fd against V6f and Gfd against Gf (the top-1 flips between two cells on one chip); the per-seed top-1 success rate (M1) of V6fd and Gfd beside V6, G, V6f and Gf; AlphaFold 3's main output (the top-ranked sample across seeds 1 to 5, per target, as AlphaFold 3 ranks it: the highest full-precision ranking score, exact ties to the lowest (seed, sample), as the post hoc analysis `winners`) in each of the four cells, over the targets with all five seeds on both sides (c_bh from V6h and Gh, seeds 1 and 2 of the main study and 3 to 5 of follow-up 12): on how many of the 60 targets it differs between v6e and the L4, and on how many its success differs, descriptive counts. The decomposition's interaction I is computed with one set of bootstrap draws over the targets common to the four cells (its four terms in one replicate each). Part B: the six pairs of {V6fd, Gfd, Afd, Hfd}; D_T for (Hfd,Afd) against HW(H,A) minus RC(H) (follow-up 9) and for (Gfd,Afd) against HW(G,A) minus RC(G) (follow-up 3), the recompilation term of the first-named condition as in follow-up 14: RC(Hfd) not measured and set to 0 (a flip rate is never negative, so "reduced" read with the term set to 0 implies "reduced" with the term measured), RC(Gfd) measured (part A), as follow-up 14 used RC(Gf) for (Gf,Af); HW(Hfd,Afd) minus HW(Hf,Af) and HW(Gfd,Afd) minus HW(Gf,Af) (follow-up 14), descriptive. Expected precision, from earlier rows: the half-width of D_T is about 5 points if c_fd is near 0 and about 7 points if it is near 13 (follow-up 13's estimate for D_F); the factorial terms carry the same order. (e) Reading rules, fixed now. (1) Reduced: the top-1 flip rate is "reduced" if the 95% interval of D_T lies below 0, "not reduced" otherwise; the same rule for the success flip rate and flips that matter, as secondary. (2) Agree: "agree" if the upper limit of the 95% interval of c_fd is below 5.0 percentage points, "agreement not shown" otherwise (follow-up 14's threshold, chosen there for the reasons its row gives; unchanged). (3) Attribution, for the top-1 flip rate, one of three statements, decided by (1) and (2) in this order (if c_fd reads "agree" while D_T reads "not reduced", the first statement is made and both readings are reported beside it): "float32 activations suffice" if c_fd reads "agree": the paper then states that between the v6e and the L4 the data type of the trunk, not the matmul precision, carries the agreement of follow-up 13, and that the remaining difference of the two operand formats (bfloat16 and TensorFloat-32) does not prevent it; "both are needed" if D_T reads "not reduced": with follow-up 12 (the precision alone does not reduce), neither change alone reduces the disagreement and only their combination does, stated as an interaction of the two settings; "both contribute" if D_T reads "reduced" and c_fd reads "agreement not shown": T_d and P_f are both reported as parts of the reduction, with their intervals, without a further split. No direction is predicted. The terms of the decomposition (T_d, P_f, I) are descriptive in every case. (4) Recompilation, descriptive: RC(Gfd) with its interval and bit-identity count (light-fetch files, the mmCIF timestamp masked, as RC(GT) in follow-up 15), and V6fd r2 as check 5. (5) Generality, part B: for each of the six pairs of {V6fd, Gfd, Afd, Hfd}, rule e2; the sentence "the float32 trunk at the default matmul precision makes the four accelerators agree" is written only if all six pairs agree, each at its own 95% level (an intersection-union test, no multiplicity correction); otherwise the pairs that agree and those that do not are named. Rule e1 for (Hfd,Afd) and (Gfd,Afd) with the terms of item d. (6) Cost, descriptive: the ratio of the median process wall time overall and per token bucket of V6fd to V6 and to V6f, Gfd to G and to Gf, Afd to A and to Af, Hfd to H and to Hf, from the run records (r1 processes); the ratio of the compiled device memory per bucket; on the TPU the allocator's peak reservation; every out-of-memory stop listed. (f) Provisioning and launch, for the shortest time to the last result; not a change in any computation. Quotas to be read on the launch day (read-only) and written in the launch row: GPUS_ALL_REGIONS; L4 on demand and Spot per region; A100 80 GB on demand and Spot per region; Spot H100 per region; TPU v6e per zone (approved and read in the console and with gcloud on 2026-11-21: GPUS_ALL_REGIONS 32; A100 80 GB on demand and preemptible, 8 in us-central1, us-east4 and us-east5; Spot H100 64 in every region and no on-demand H100 quota; L4 16 per region, on demand and preemptible; CPUs 3,000 in all regions; v6e as below). With 32 GPUs the wrappers of follow-ups 17 to 21 run in the order and with the MAX values of the launch plan (`COSTI_E_CALENDARIO.md`, kept outside the repository): the sum of the MAX values of the GPU wrappers running at once stays at or below 32. v6e on Flex-start (as V6f of follow-up 13; `V6E_PROVISIONING=flex_start`), up to four VMs at a time on different zones (`MAX_V6E=4`, Lorenzo's choice of 2026-11-21; the project's v6e quota, read in the console on 2026-11-21, is 512 on-demand and 1,536 preemptible cores per zone in europe-west4-a, -b, -c and us-east5-a, -b; in follow-up 13 capacity, not quota, was missing), the zones tried in that order with a Flex-start request valid 15 min in each (`ZONES` and `REQUEST_VALID_FOR=15m` on every line); a part that obtains no Flex-start VM in any of them, or whose VM is lost once, is relaunched on demand over the same zones (below); `cloud/prices.csv` has no v6e price for us-east5, which the launcher prices at europe-west4's (its printed note). L4 on Spot (`SPOT=1`, `MAX_L4=20`), the launcher's L4 zones with us-east4-a and -c last. Part B: A100 on Spot (`MAX_A100=12`, `A100_REGION_CAPS="us-east5:8 us-central1:8 us-east4:8"`), H100 on Spot (`MAX_H100=8`, zones us-central1-a, -b, -c, us-east5-a, us-east4-a, -b, -c): the A100 and the H100 over the three regions of the approved quotas (us-central1, us-east5, us-east4), us-east4 last on every GPU (setup failures of 2026-11-12), where a setup failure before any AlphaFold 3 run is relaunched unchanged. A part whose Spot VM is preempted or lost once (Spot preemption, VM gone, maintenance event) is relaunched at once on demand (`PROVISIONING=standard` on its line, written by the retry wrapper `prep_retry_arm.sh` and by the supervisor, both outside the repository), where the project has an on-demand quota for that accelerator (L4, A100, v6e; the project has no on-demand H100 quota, console of 2026-11-21, so an H100 part is relaunched on Spot again, as in follow-ups 9, 14 and 15); every later launch of that part is on demand; each fallback is listed in the completion row (the speed of the results comes before their cost: decision of 2026-11-21). A part that obtained no VM is retried unchanged. Parts hold whole targets (`harness/plans/make_prep_plans.py --label dtp`: V6fd 4 parts, V6fd r2 1, Gfd 60 of one target each, Afd 12, Hfd 8), each at most 8 h (TPU) or 4 h (GPU) of job at the worst factor where a single target allows it, the number of parts of each arm the one that minimises its wrapper's expected duration on its MAX slots with the arm's expected cost at most 10% above that of the fewest parts the job limit allows (rule of 2026-11-21, `make_prep_plans.py`); deadline ceil(worst job min x 1.25 + 20). Every wrapper's `LAUNCHER` is `scripts/monitor/prep_guarded_launch.sh` (item g). Part A's v6e and L4 wrappers start together; part B starts after part A, through its gate. A supervisor outside the repository (`~/Desktop/af3_drafts/prep_crediti_20261121/prep_supervisor.sh dtp` with `prep_sup_state.py`, copies of follow-up 15's generalised to the label, and the retry wrapper `prep_retry_arm.sh`, a copy of `retry_arm_par.sh` with the fallback; their SHA-256 in the launch row) relaunches the parts whose last session failed for a transient reason (no VM or setup failure before any AlphaFold 3 run: unchanged; Spot preemption or VM gone: on demand, by the fallback rule above; at most 4 rounds per platform; none once the stop marker exists) and applies the gate of part B when part A has ended. The provisioning model, the zone and the image enter no contrast. `FETCH=full` for V6fd r2; the light fetch otherwise. The launches run under `caffeinate -dims` from the main checkout at the commit that merges the implementation. (g) Cost and budget. From `cloud/prices.csv` and the median process wall time per bucket of the float32-trunk arms at the highest precision on the same hardware (V6f 123.6, 151.8, 209.8 and 268.2 s; Gf 156.4, 340.4, 665.5 and 1,209.6 s, follow-up 13; Af 119.3, 193.3, 322.5 and 508.7 s; Hf 76.2, 111.5, 172.5 and 264.2 s, follow-up 14, for buckets 256 to 1,024), an upper estimate because the default precision runs fewer passes, and their per-part overheads, times 1.0 expected and 1.25 worst. Prices, the highest row of the planned provisioning among the regions each wrapper uses: v6e Flex-start 1.35 USD per chip hour (on demand 2.97), L4 Spot 0.5383 USD/h (on demand 0.8972), A100 Spot 3.0166 (on demand 5.6625, us-east4), H100 Spot 6.5874 (us-east4; on demand 12.3658). Part A: V6fd 300 processes, 15.36 VM h, 20.75 USD expected (25.59 worst); V6fd r2 10 processes, 0.77 VM h, 1.04 USD (1.21); Gfd 600 processes, 95.61 VM h, 51.40 USD (62.11; on demand 85.88 and 103.59); together 73.19 USD expected, 88.91 worst. Part B: Afd 300 processes, 22.19 VM h, 66.93 USD (82.11); Hfd 300 processes, 12.47 VM h, 82.24 USD (100.74); together 149.17 USD expected, 182.85 worst. Follow-up 17 in all 222.36 USD expected, 271.76 worst without preemption. With the fallback on demand after one preemption (item f), the worst case of each part includes a fallback allowance: its planned worst case (the VM lost at the end of its worst job) plus a share of its worst case at the fallback's price (on demand; Spot again for the H100), the share of parts assumed to fall back (L4 0.75, A100 0.5, H100 0.25, v6e 1.0; the shares of parts that lost a Spot VM in the run records up to 2026-11-15 were about 0.5, 0.25 and 0.1, and the v6e lacked Flex-start capacity for hours in follow-up 13); the realistic estimate takes shares of 0.5, 0.25, 0.1 and 0.5, the VM lost halfway through the expected job and the part rerun at the fallback's expected cost (`scripts/monitor/prep_budget.py costs`): part A 121.79 USD realistic, 225.53 worst with fallback; part B 176.31 and 285.10; in all 298.10 realistic, 510.63 worst with fallback (689.14 if every part lost its VM once at the end of its worst job and reran at the fallback's price). Follow-ups 14 and 15 lost 3% to 12% of their spend to Spot sessions that did not complete. Budget, on the Google TPU Builders credit only (the Free Trial credit is not counted). Cap of follow-up 17: 590 USD (its worst case with fallback times 1.15, rounded up to 5 USD). Budget rule before each wrapper (`scripts/monitor/credit_check_prep.sh dtp v6e|l4|a100|h100 BALANCE`, `scripts/monitor/prep_budget.py`): GO if the balance read from the console, minus what every other running wrapper of follow-ups 17 to 21 may still spend (its worst case with fallback less its spend), minus this wrapper's worst case with fallback, stays at or above 25 USD; and follow-up 17's spend so far, plus what its other running wrappers may still spend, plus this wrapper's worst case with fallback, stays within 590 USD; and, for part B, every part A part has a complete session and no follow-up 17 session is running; and the dates of item h. Part A is never held back for part B: part B runs only from what is left (the TPU Builders credit of 1,000 USD for follow-ups 17 to 21 arrived on 2026-11-23, beside 23.94 USD left of the earlier credit; part B is expected to run). Stop rule, before every part (`scripts/monitor/prep_guarded_launch.sh`, which runs `prep_budget.py guard` and then `cloud/af3_run.sh` unchanged): the projected spend is follow-up 17's spend so far from the session records (VM running to deletion at the list price of its provisioning) plus what every other part still needs plus this part's expected cost (on demand for a fallback); if it exceeds 590 USD, the part is not launched (no VM is created), the wrapper starts no further part, the reason is written to `results/dtp_cap_stop.txt`, every later part is refused while that file exists, and Lorenzo decides in a row; running parts finish. A part B part is also refused while a part A part has no complete session. Every gate refuses a launch after 2026-12-06 or an expected end after 2026-12-08. Download to the Mac: the light fetch of 1,210 processes, about 2.5 GB (main-study median per process by bucket 1.0 to 3.7 MB), and the full outputs of V6fd r2 and of the 10 V6fd r1 processes it is compared with, about 0.5 GB. (h) Duration and calendar. From the first VM to the last result of each wrapper, at the expected factor and without capacity waits: v6e 4.6 h (5.6 h worst) with four VMs at once, L4 5.1 h (6.1 h) with twenty; part B, A100 1.9 h (2.4 h) with twelve and H100 1.6 h (2.0 h) with eight; follow-ups 17 to 21 together about 11 h (14 h worst) from the first VM to the last result, in the launch order of `COSTI_E_CALENDARIO.md`. Known capacity risks: v6e Flex-start capacity has not been tried since 2026-11-12; Spot A100 capacity waits of up to 19 attempts for one part (follow-up 14); Spot L4 preemptions of 5 of 8 parts at once in europe-west4-a (follow-up 15). The implementation is committed and the OSF addendum registered before the first run; hard limits: launch by 2026-12-06, every run finished by 2026-12-08 (`launch_by` and `finish_by` in every plan), else the arm is reported as incomplete; analysis by 2026-12-10. No run is dropped or selected on results; an interrupted part is relaunched unchanged; every launch, relaunch, fallback, gate and stop is recorded in section 10. (i) Already seen before this row: every registered result of the main study and follow-ups 1 to 16, among them the three cells c_bd (HW(V6,G) 20.7% [16.3, 25.3]), c_bh (HWh(V6,G) 20.7% [16.0, 25.7], five seeds) and c_fh (HW(V6f,Gf) 0.3% [0.0, 1.0]), RC(G) 7.3%, RC(Gf) 0.0%, D_F -13.0 [-17.7, -8.3], PR(V6) 20.0% and PR(G) 10.3% (five seeds), follow-up 14's ten float32 pairs (0.3% to 2.0%) and its cost ratios, F(V5), F(A) and F(H), and the post hoc analyses up to 2026-11-21 (`returned`, `rare`, `rounded`, `winners`, `cpu_matched`, `winners_ext`, `pb_flips`, `best25`, `gtat`). | 2026-11-24 | Follow-up experiment 18, AlphaFold 3's default Triton attention on the H100 and the A100 (label `tri`), specified after the main-study and follow-up 1 to 16 outcomes and the post hoc analyses up to 2026-11-21 were read (item i) and before any run; reported as a follow-up, outside the pre-registered study, whatever it shows. Questions. (T1) Follow-up 9 found that the H100 and the A100 rank different samples first in 20.7% [16.3, 25.0] of (target, seed) predictions, 10.7 points [6.3, 15.0] more often than a recompilation on the H100 (HW(H,A) minus RC(H)), with AlphaFold 3's portable XLA attention on both. AlphaFold 3's default on a GPU is its Triton flash attention, and on the L4 and the A100 the change of attention alone changed the top-ranked sample in 13.7% and 12.3% of predictions (DU, main study; DU(A), follow-up 3). With the default attention on both GPUs, does the H100 against A100 difference still exceed recompilation? (T2) With the default attention, how often does the main output that AlphaFold 3 returns for a target (the top-ranked of the 25 samples of seeds 1 to 5, as AlphaFold 3 ranks across seeds) differ between the two GPU types, how often does its success differ, which success criterion changes and by how much, and how does that compare with moving the same job to another VM of the same GPU type? (T3) What do the recompilation of the A100 Triton arm and the default-configuration pairs among the GPUs (L4 Triton, A100 Triton, H100 Triton) show? (a) Settings. One new configuration appended to `harness/configs.yaml`, none changed: `h100_triton`, the environment of `h100_xla` (follow-up 9) with `flash_attention_implementation: triton`, as `a100_triton` is `a100_xla` with it and `l4_triton` is `l4_xla` with it; AlphaFold 3's `docs/performance.md` names the H100 80 GB with the A100 80 GB as tested with these settings. Every arm keeps the settings of its source arm: AlphaFold 3 v3.0.4 with `af3_tpu/af3_tpu.patch`, the same weights, software stack (jax, jaxlib, jax-cuda12-plugin and -pjrt 0.10.2), frozen inputs of `inputs/manifest.csv`, 10 recycles, 5 samples, bfloat16 trunk at the default precision, `--xla_gpu_enable_triton_gemm=false`, preallocation with memory fraction 0.95, machine types `a3-highgpu-1g` and `a2-ultragpu-1g`, the Eigen archive, one fresh process per (target, seed, repetition) with an empty compile cache. No AlphaFold 3 file, no file of `af3_tpu/`, `src/`, `cloud/`, `inputs/`, `targets/` and no harness module changes. Images: HT r1 and HT r2 on the project family `af2-tpu-benchmark/af3-accel-2204-nvidia-580` (image `af3-accel-2204-nvidia-580-v20261104a`, NVIDIA driver 580.178.04). AT r1 (follow-up 3, sessions `20261018T002401Z`, `025226Z` and `050905Z`, VMs created on 2026-10-18 between 00:24Z and 05:10Z) ran on Canonical's family `ubuntu-os-accelerator-images/ubuntu-accelerator-2204-amd64-with-nvidia-580`, whose newest image then was, by the family's history, `ubuntu-accelerator-2204-amd64-with-nvidia-580-v20261016` (the image is not recorded per session; inferred as for GT r1 in follow-up 15); before part B Lorenzo reads whether that image is still available (read-only) and, if so, copies it into the project as image `af3-accel-2204-nvidia-580-v20261016`, family `af3-accel-2204-nvidia-580-atr1`, and AT r2 is created from it; if not, AT r2 runs on the project family above and the difference is reported by check 4. The machine setup installs CPython 3.12.16 where AT r1 and H had 3.12.15 (row of 2026-11-14 on the Python patch release); check 4 records it. (b) Arms. Part A (mandatory): HT (H100, `h100_triton`), the 60 targets, seeds 1 to 5, r1; HT r2, the same, r2, in parts of their own, so that every r2 process runs on a VM other than its r1's (as H r2 in follow-up 9; six VMs for r1 and six others for r2). Part B (conditional, item g): AT r2 (A100, `a100_triton`), the 60 targets, seeds 1 to 5, r2, on new VMs; its r1 is follow-up 3's AT. Used as they are: H r1 and r2 (follow-up 9), A r1 and r2 and AT r1 (follow-up 3), G, GT r1 (main study), GT r2 (follow-up 15). A smoke test before part A (label `smoke`, an engineering check, never a finding or a contrast): 7U3J, seed 1, once with `h100_triton` (`harness/plans/tri_smoke/tri_smoke_ht.yaml`), because Triton attention has never run on an H100 in this study; if its wall time exceeds 1.5 times the H median of the 256-token bucket (64.8 s), the parts are regenerated with the measured factor, recorded in a row, before any part A part is launched. Out-of-memory stops and other failures are recorded with their cause, never hidden or rerun with other settings; a (target, seed) missing on either side of a contrast leaves it (section 8) and the counts are reported. (c) Checks, before any result is read: (1) every process exits 0 or its failure is recorded; (2) one machine type per condition, AT r2's that of AT r1 (`a2-ultragpu-1g`) and HT's `a3-highgpu-1g`; (3) completeness per condition, repetition and seed; (4) settings and stack: `run_alphafold` called with `--flash_attention_implementation=triton` in every HT and AT r2 process (the harness's command record), the `command` of `runs.jsonl`; no `AF3_FU13_*` variable and no `JAX_DEFAULT_MATMUL_PRECISION` in any (`session.json`: `xla_env`, `env_overrides` and the configuration's `env`) and no float32 trunk recorded in `af3_entry_stats.json`, every process on the expected device (NVIDIA H100 80GB HBM3, NVIDIA A100-SXM4-80GB); for every session, the driver, kernel, Python version, `pip_freeze.txt` and the image family (as the wrapper log printed it; "not recorded" otherwise) against those of H (for HT) and AT r1 (for AT r2), any difference listed; (5) every HT r2 session ran on a VM whose name differs from that of the session of the same target in HT r1 (from the launch records). The analysis stops if check 1 or 2 fails. (d) Statistics: as section 5, the target-clustered bootstrap with 10,000 replicates and seed 2026, every contrast paired on (target, seed) with the per-seed top-1 (M2), seeds 1 to 5, all terms of a difference on the same resampled targets. Primary (T1): HW(HT,AT) and E_T = HW(HT,AT) minus RC(HT), HT the first-named condition, as follow-up 9's HW(H,A) minus RC(H); RC(HT) = HT r1 against HT r2 (new VMs). Co-primary (T2), on the 60 targets: the main output of each target, over the seeds both sides ran (the (seed, sample) with the highest full-precision ranking score among the 25 samples of seeds 1 to 5) under HT r1, HT r2 and AT; MO(HT,AT), the fraction of targets whose main output is a different (seed, sample) under HT and AT, and MO(HT), the same between HT r1 and HT r2; E_MO = MO(HT,AT) minus MO(HT); the fraction of targets whose main output's success differs (M1), with its recompilation counterpart and their difference; for every target whose main output differs: the ligand RMSD between the two returned ligands after pocket superposition (M4), each one's ligand RMSD from the crystal, whether both, one or neither is within 2 Å of the crystal pose (ligand RMSD below 2 Å, the M1 threshold), and, where success differs, which PoseBusters check or the 2 Å criterion changes and by how much (the method of the post hoc analysis `pb_flips`, row of 2026-11-16: the criterion and the failing checks from the saved M1, the excess over each tolerance from a rerun of M1 with PoseBusters' full report that must first reproduce the saved M1 exactly, else the analysis stops; `reproduce.sh` runs it by default); the same for HT r1 against HT r2. Secondary: the success flip rate and flips that matter of HW(HT,AT) and their excess over RC(HT); E_T against the larger of RC(HT) and RC(AT) (the larger chosen per statistic by the point estimates on the full sample, as Table III; the excess and its interval on the targets common to HW(HT,AT) and that RC; with part B only); RC(HT) and RC(AT) themselves (same GPU type, different VMs) with their bit-identity counts (the light-fetch files, the mmCIF timestamp masked, as RC(GT) in follow-up 15); RC(AT) beside RC(A) (10.0%, same VM) and RC(HT) beside RC(H) (9.3%, new VMs), and their differences; HW(HT,AT) minus HW(H,A) (the attention's effect on the pair); DU(H) = H r1 against HT r1, beside DU(A) and DU (L4). Descriptive, the default-configuration pairs among the GPUs (T3; AlphaFold 3's default attention on each): HW(GT,AT), HW(GT,HT) and HW(HT,AT), each with HW minus the larger of the two RC rates; HW(GT,AT) uses only existing outputs (main study and follow-up 3) and was computed before this row by the post hoc analysis `gtat` (row of 2026-11-21): here it is not a new contrast but a declared replication of that reading, recomputed from the same seed pairs with the same bootstrap and required to equal the registered `gtat` values (a reproduction check, reported; a difference is reported and the `gtat` values stand); its excess over the larger RC rate uses RC(AT) from part B, which `gtat` did not have. Per-seed top-1 success rate (M1) of HT beside H, A and AT. Cost: the ratio of the median process wall time overall and per bucket, HT to H and AT r2 to AT r1, and of the compiled device memory. Expected precision: HW(H,A) minus RC(H) was 10.7 [6.3, 15.0] (half-width about 4.4 points); MO on 60 targets has steps of 1.67 points and a half-width of about 10 to 12 points (follow-up 9's main output changed on 20 of 60 targets), so T2 can show a large excess and will not show a small one. (e) Reading rules, fixed now. (1) T1, top-1 flip rate: "beyond recompilation" if the 95% interval of E_T lies above 0, "not distinguishable from recompilation" otherwise (not a test of equivalence), as in section 5 and the paper's Table III; the same for the success flip rate and flips that matter, as secondary. The paper's sentences on the A100 and the H100, in the abstract ("Between the A100 and H100 it exceeded recompilation, ...") and in Section V-A, are kept as written only if E_T reads "beyond recompilation"; otherwise each is qualified as holding with XLA attention and not shown with the default attention. (2) T2, main output: "the main output differs beyond same-type rescheduling" if the 95% interval of E_MO lies above 0, "not distinguishable from same-type rescheduling" otherwise; the same rule for the main output's success, as secondary; the counts of targets in each category of item d (both poses within 2 Å, one, neither; the criterion that changes) are reported without a threshold. (3) Larger reference (with part B): E_T against the larger of RC(HT) and RC(AT) is read with rule 1 and reported beside it; if the two readings differ, both are stated. (4) Recompilation across VMs, descriptive: RC(HT), RC(AT) with their intervals and bit-identity counts; nothing is attributed to the VM change, because no arm here has a same-VM recompilation (follow-up 21 measures that). (5) Default-configuration pairs, descriptive: HW(GT,AT) (the replication of `gtat`), HW(GT,HT) and HW(HT,AT) with their intervals and their excess over the larger RC rate, reported in one table beside the XLA pairs HW(G,A), HW(H,G) and HW(H,A) with their excess over the larger of their registered RC rates; for a default pair whose second RC rate was not measured (RC(AT) without part B) the excess uses the measured one, named; HW(HT,AT) keeps its pre-specified primary reading of rule 1, HW(GT,AT) only the reading of `gtat`. No direction is predicted. (f) Provisioning and launch, for the shortest time to the last result; not a change in any computation. Quotas to be read on the launch day (read-only) and written in the launch row: GPUS_ALL_REGIONS; Spot H100 per region; A100 80 GB on demand and Spot per region (approved and read in the console and with gcloud on 2026-11-21: GPUS_ALL_REGIONS 32; A100 80 GB on demand and preemptible, 8 in us-central1, us-east4 and us-east5; Spot H100 64 in every region and no on-demand H100 quota; L4 16 per region, on demand and preemptible; CPUs 3,000 in all regions). The wrappers of follow-ups 17 to 21 run in the order and with the MAX values of the launch plan (`COSTI_E_CALENDARIO.md`, outside the repository), at most 32 GPUs at once. The smoke test on Spot H100 first (`cloud/af3_run.sh` with the smoke plan, one VM). Part A: one wrapper with the 10 parts of HT and HT r2, Spot, `MAX_H100=12`, zones us-central1-a, -b, -c, us-east5-a and us-east4-a, -b, -c (us-east4 last: setup failures of 2026-11-12, relaunched unchanged); an HT r2 part may run at the same time as an HT r1 part (different VMs; check 5). Part B: AT r2, Spot, `MAX_A100=12`, `A100_REGION_CAPS="us-east5:8 us-central1:8 us-east4:8"` (the three regions of the approved quota, us-east4 last, where a setup failure before any AlphaFold 3 run is relaunched unchanged), after part A through its gate. A part whose Spot VM is preempted or lost once (Spot preemption, VM gone, maintenance event) is relaunched at once on demand (`PROVISIONING=standard` on its line, written by the retry wrapper `prep_retry_arm.sh` and by the supervisor, both outside the repository), where the project has an on-demand quota for that accelerator (L4, A100, v6e; the project has no on-demand H100 quota, console of 2026-11-21, so an H100 part is relaunched on Spot again, as in follow-ups 9, 14 and 15); every later launch of that part is on demand; each fallback is listed in the completion row (the speed of the results comes before their cost: decision of 2026-11-21). A part that obtained no VM is retried unchanged. Parts hold whole targets (`harness/plans/make_prep_plans.py --label tri`: HT 5, HT r2 5, AT r2 7, the number of parts of each arm the one that minimises its wrapper's expected duration on its MAX slots with the arm's expected cost at most 10% above that of the fewest parts the job limit allows (rule of 2026-11-21, `make_prep_plans.py`)); deadline ceil(worst job min x 1.25 + 20). Every wrapper's `LAUNCHER` is `scripts/monitor/prep_guarded_launch.sh`; the supervisor `prep_supervisor.sh tri` (outside the repository, as for follow-up 17) relaunches transient failures and applies part B's gate. The light fetch for every arm (as AT r1 and GT). The provisioning model, the zone and the image enter no contrast. Launches under `caffeinate -dims` from the main checkout at the commit that merges the implementation. (g) Cost and budget. From `cloud/prices.csv` and the median process wall time per bucket of the source arm (H, follow-up 9: 64.8, 75.4, 98.3 and 136.8 s; AT, follow-up 3: 95.0, 116.0, 136.8 and 172.4 s, for buckets 256 to 1,024) and their per-part overheads, times 1.0 expected and 1.5 worst for HT (Triton never measured on the H100; on the A100 it took 0.74 to 0.93 times the XLA time by bucket) and 1.25 worst for AT r2. Prices: H100 Spot 6.5874 USD/h (us-east4; on demand 12.3658), A100 Spot 3.0166 (on demand 5.6625, us-east4). Part A: HT 300 processes, 8.91 VM h, 58.60 USD expected (82.10 worst); HT r2 the same; together 117.20 USD expected, 164.20 worst. Part B: AT r2 300 processes, 12.61 VM h, 38.07 USD (45.72). Follow-up 18 in all 155.27 USD expected, 209.92 worst without preemption; the smoke test about 1 USD. With the fallback on demand after one preemption (item f), the worst case of each part includes a fallback allowance: its planned worst case (the VM lost at the end of its worst job) plus a share of its worst case at the fallback's price (on demand; Spot again for the H100), the share of parts assumed to fall back (L4 0.75, A100 0.5, H100 0.25, v6e 1.0; the shares of parts that lost a Spot VM in the run records up to 2026-11-15 were about 0.5, 0.25 and 0.1, and the v6e lacked Flex-start capacity for hours in follow-up 13); the realistic estimate takes shares of 0.5, 0.25, 0.1 and 0.5, the VM lost halfway through the expected job and the part rerun at the fallback's expected cost (`scripts/monitor/prep_budget.py costs`): part A 123.06 USD realistic, 205.25 worst with fallback; part B 51.16 and 88.62; in all 174.22 realistic, 293.87 worst with fallback (459.92 if every part lost its VM once at the end of its worst job and reran at the fallback's price). Cap of follow-up 18: 340 USD (its worst case with fallback times 1.15, rounded up to 5 USD). Budget rule before each wrapper (`scripts/monitor/credit_check_prep.sh tri h100|a100 BALANCE`) and stop rule before every part (`scripts/monitor/prep_guarded_launch.sh`), as item g of follow-up 17 with this follow-up's cap and marker (`results/tri_cap_stop.txt`): GO only if the balance minus what every other running wrapper of follow-ups 17 to 21 may still spend minus this wrapper's worst case with fallback stays at or above 25 USD, the follow-up's projected spend stays within 340 USD, part A has ended for part B, and the dates. Download to the Mac: the light fetch of 900 processes, about 2 GB. (h) Duration and calendar. From the first VM, at the expected factor and without capacity waits: part A 1.8 h (2.5 h worst) with twelve H100 at a time; part B 1.9 h (2.2 h) with twelve A100. Known capacity risks: H100 Spot ran without preemption in follow-ups 9, 14 and 15 but lost three VMs at once after 8 min on 2026-11-12; Spot A100 needed up to 19 attempts for one part (follow-up 14). The implementation is committed and the OSF addendum registered before the first run (smoke test included); hard limits: launch by 2026-12-06, every run finished by 2026-12-08, else the arm is reported as incomplete; analysis by 2026-12-10. No run is dropped or selected on results; an interrupted part is relaunched unchanged; every launch, relaunch, gate and stop is recorded in section 10. (i) Already seen before this row: every registered result of the main study and follow-ups 1 to 16, among them HW(H,A) 20.7%, RC(H) 9.3%, RC(A) 10.0%, HW(H,A) minus RC(H) 10.7 [6.3, 15.0], DU 13.7% and DU(A) 12.3% [8.7, 16.3], AT's own contrasts in follow-up 3 (DU(A), Q3 with AT), GT's (HW(V5,GT), HW(V6,GT), HW(GT,C), SD(GT), RC(GT) 7.7%), follow-up 9's main output (20 of 60 targets), and the post hoc analyses up to 2026-11-21 (`winners`, `winners_ext`, `pb_flips`, `best25` among them, none of which uses AT against GT or an H100 Triton arm, and `gtat`, the L4 Triton against the A100 Triton arm, HW(GT,AT) with its reading against RC(GT), specified and computed on 2026-11-21). Not seen: no H100 Triton process exists; AT has no recompilation. To prepare this row only run records were read (process wall times and overheads of H and AT, through `make_prep_plans.py`) and the registered tables named above. (j) Code: `harness/configs.yaml` (`h100_triton` appended), `harness/plans/make_prep_plans.py` (plans `harness/plans/tri/`, `tri_parts.csv`, `tri_table.md`, parts files `results/parts_tri_h100.txt`, `parts_tri_a100.txt`), `harness/plans/tri_smoke/tri_smoke_ht.yaml`, `harness/plans/config_diff_tri.py`, `scripts/monitor/prep_budget.py`, `credit_check_prep.sh`, `prep_guarded_launch.sh`, `analysis/tri.py` (with the option `--gtat`, the output folder of `gtat` that HW(GT,AT) is compared with, and `--pb_full`) with `analysis/tests/test_tri.py`, `scripts/analysis_runs/tri/` (`reproduce.sh`, `check_sessions.py`, `sessions.txt`), `analysis/tests/test_eigen.py` (folders `tri` and `tri_smoke`); recorded with their SHA-256 in the implementation row. | The A100 and H100 result, the paper's strongest case for a scheduler move, was measured with XLA attention, not AlphaFold 3's default on GPUs; the reviewers ask for it with the default attention, across VMs of the same type, and on the main output |
| 2026-11-24 | Follow-up experiment 19, recompilation of the float32 A100 and H100 arms on the other 50 targets (label `rcf`), specified after the main-study and follow-up 1 to 16 outcomes and the post hoc analyses up to 2026-11-21 were read (item i) and before any run; reported as a follow-up, outside the pre-registered study, whatever it shows. Question. Follow-up 15 measured the recompilation of follow-up 14's float32 arms on the A100 and the H100 on the 10 pilot targets only (RC(Af) and RC(Hf) 0.0% of 50 (target, seed) each, none bit-identical), so Table IV gives them as pilot values and follow-up 14's D_F of (Hf,Af) is read with RC(Hf) measured on 10 targets. On all 60 targets, how often does recompilation change the top-ranked sample of Af and of Hf? (a) Settings: as follow-up 15's part A4, unchanged: `a100_xla_fu14_f32` on `a2-ultragpu-1g` and `h100_xla_fu14_f32` on `a3-highgpu-1g`, `rep_start: 2`, new VMs, the project image family `af2-tpu-benchmark/af3-accel-2204-nvidia-580` (the image of follow-up 15's r2 and of the last Af r1 parts); no file of the computation changes; the differences of image from Af r1 and Hf r1 are those follow-up 15's row lists. (b) Arms: Af r2 and Hf r2 on the 50 non-pilot targets, seeds 1 to 5 (250 processes each). With follow-up 15's r2 of the 10 pilot targets they make r2 of all 60. (c) Checks, before any result is read: (1) exit codes; (2) one machine type per condition, the r1's; (3) completeness, the follow-up 19 processes on the 50 non-pilot targets only (a pilot target among them is listed and left out: its r2 is follow-up 15's) and follow-up 15's r2 of the 10 pilot targets; (4) `global_config_bfloat16: none` and `JAX_DEFAULT_MATMUL_PRECISION=highest` in every process and the expected device; driver, kernel, Python, packages, `pip_freeze.txt`, configuration entry and environment (`session.json`) and image family (the wrapper log) against Af r1 and Hf r1, any difference listed; (5) RC(Af) and RC(Hf) recomputed on the 10 pilot targets equal follow-up 15's seed pairs unit by unit, reported. The analysis stops if check 1 or 2 fails. (d) Statistics: section 5's bootstrap (10,000 replicates, seed 2026), per-seed top-1, seeds 1 to 5. RC(Af) and RC(Hf) on the 60 targets (r1 of follow-up 14 against r2 of follow-ups 15 and 19), and on the 50 new targets alone; the three flip statistics, M4 and M5 medians, bit-identical fraction (the light-fetch files on the 50, every file on the 10, as follow-up 15). D_F of (Hf,Af) with RC(Hf) measured on 60 targets, for the three flip statistics, with the same 10,000 draws as the registered D_F (follow-up 14's `d_f.csv`, recomputed first and required to match: if it does not match, every output is written, the report says so and the analysis exits with status 1); the draws are the same only if RC(Hf) covers every target of the registered D_F, which the report states. (e) Reading rules, none beyond follow-up 15's: the rates with their intervals fill Table IV on 60 targets; the registered D_F reading ("reduced", -10.3 [-15.3, -5.3]) stands whatever this shows, and the report states whether D_F with RC(Hf) measured on 60 targets would read the same under rule e1 of follow-up 14. (f) Provisioning: Spot, `MAX_A100=12` with `A100_REGION_CAPS="us-east5:8 us-central1:8 us-east4:8"` (the A100 80 GB quota approved on 2026-11-21, on demand and preemptible: 8 in us-central1, us-east5 and us-east4; us-east4 last, where a setup failure before any AlphaFold 3 run is relaunched unchanged), `MAX_H100=10` with zones us-central1-a, -b, -c, us-east5-a and us-east4-a, -b, -c (us-east4 last); a part whose Spot VM is preempted or lost once (Spot preemption, VM gone, maintenance event) is relaunched at once on demand (`PROVISIONING=standard` on its line, written by the retry wrapper `prep_retry_arm.sh` and by the supervisor, both outside the repository), where the project has an on-demand quota for that accelerator (L4, A100, v6e; the project has no on-demand H100 quota, console of 2026-11-21, so an H100 part is relaunched on Spot again, as in follow-ups 9, 14 and 15); every later launch of that part is on demand; each fallback is listed in the completion row (the speed of the results comes before their cost: decision of 2026-11-21). A part that obtained no VM is retried unchanged; parts of whole targets (`make_prep_plans.py --label rcf`: Af 12, Hf 9, the number of parts of each arm the one that minimises its wrapper's expected duration on its MAX slots with the arm's expected cost at most 10% above that of the fewest parts the job limit allows (rule of 2026-11-21, `make_prep_plans.py`)); the light fetch; guarded launcher and supervisor as follow-up 17. The wrappers of follow-ups 17 to 21 run in the order and with the MAX values of the launch plan (`COSTI_E_CALENDARIO.md`, outside the repository): GPUS_ALL_REGIONS 32 (approved on 2026-11-21), at most 32 GPUs at once. (g) Cost: from the Af and Hf medians per bucket (follow-up 14) and their overheads, times 1.0 and 1.25: Af r2 55.38 USD expected (67.74 worst), Hf r2 69.45 (84.52); 124.83 expected, 152.26 worst without preemption; with the fallback of item f, the worst case of each part includes a fallback allowance: its planned worst case (the VM lost at the end of its worst job) plus a share of its worst case at the fallback's price (on demand; Spot again for the H100), the share of parts assumed to fall back (L4 0.75, A100 0.5, H100 0.25, v6e 1.0; the shares of parts that lost a Spot VM in the run records up to 2026-11-15 were about 0.5, 0.25 and 0.1, and the v6e lacked Flex-start capacity for hours in follow-up 13); the realistic estimate takes shares of 0.5, 0.25, 0.1 and 0.5, the VM lost halfway through the expected job and the part rerun at the fallback's expected cost (`scripts/monitor/prep_budget.py costs`): 147.38 realistic, 236.95 worst with fallback; cap 275 USD (the worst case with fallback times 1.15, rounded up to 5 USD); budget and stop rules as follow-up 17 (`credit_check_prep.sh rcf a100|h100 BALANCE`, `results/rcf_cap_stop.txt`). (h) Duration: A100 1.6 h (2.0 h worst) with twelve VMs, H100 1.2 h (1.5 h) with ten, the two wrappers together. Launch by 2026-12-06, finished by 2026-12-08, analysis by 2026-12-10. No run is dropped or selected on results. (i) Already seen: every registered result up to follow-up 16, among them follow-up 15's RC(Af) and RC(Hf) on the pilot targets and the D_F sensitivity. Only run records were read. (j) Code: `make_prep_plans.py` (plans `harness/plans/rcf/`, `rcf_parts.csv`, `rcf_table.md`), `prep_budget.py`, `analysis/rcf.py` with `analysis/tests/test_rcf.py` (an extension of `analysis/rcx.py` to the 60 targets; it uses the helpers of `analysis/xvm.py`), `scripts/analysis_runs/rcf/` (`reproduce.sh`, which first runs `scripts/analysis_runs/rcx/fetch_full_outputs.py --check` for the pilot targets' full outputs, `check_sessions.py`, `sessions.txt`), `analysis/tests/test_eigen.py` (folder `rcf`). | Table IV gives the float32 recompilation of the A100 and the H100 on 10 pilot targets only |
| 2026-11-24 | Follow-up experiment 20, the L4 without autotuning (label `aut`), specified after the main-study and follow-up 1 to 16 outcomes and the post hoc analyses up to 2026-11-21 were read (item i) and before any run; reported as a follow-up, outside the pre-registered study, whatever it shows. Questions. On the L4 a recompilation changes the top-ranked sample in 7.3% [4.7, 10.3] of predictions (RC(G), main study) and XLA's deterministic-ops flag removes it (RC(Gd) 0 of 300, every sample bit-identical); the paper attributes the recompilation differences to kernel timing at compile time on the evidence of a diagnostic on two targets (row of 2026-10-11, `diag`). (U1) With XLA's GPU autotuning turned off (`--xla_gpu_autotune_level=0`), is recompilation on the L4 bit-identical, and how often does it change the top-ranked sample, on the 60 targets? (U2) How far does turning autotuning off move the results from G, and at what cost in time? (a) Settings: the existing configuration `l4_xla_noautotune` (`l4_xla` with `--xla_gpu_autotune_level=0` appended to `XLA_FLAGS`), unchanged; every other setting as G of the main study (AlphaFold 3 v3.0.4 with the patch, weights, stack, frozen inputs, recycles, samples, `g2-standard-8`, XLA attention, bfloat16 trunk at the default precision, preallocation, the Eigen archive, one fresh process per (target, seed, repetition) with an empty compile cache); image family `af2-tpu-benchmark/af3-accel-2204-nvidia-580-gtr1` (the copy of the image of G r1, as follow-up 21), else the project family, reported by check 4. No AlphaFold 3 file, no file of `af3_tpu/`, `src/`, `cloud/`, `inputs/`, `targets/`, no harness module and no configuration changes. (b) Arms: Gna, the 60 targets, seeds 1 to 5, r1 and r2 in the same part (one VM, as G and Gd of the main study). (c) Checks, before any result is read: (1) exit codes; (2) one machine type, G's (`g2-standard-8`); (3) completeness; (4) `--xla_gpu_autotune_level=0` as a word of `XLA_FLAGS` in `xla_env` of every process (`session.json`) and the device NVIDIA L4; driver, kernel, Python, packages, `pip_freeze.txt`, configuration entry and environment and image family against G's sessions, any difference listed (the flag itself and the Python patch release, 3.12.16 against 3.12.15, are expected). The analysis stops if check 1 or 2 fails. (d) Statistics: section 5's bootstrap (10,000 replicates, seed 2026), per-seed top-1, seeds 1 to 5. RC(Gna) = Gna r1 against Gna r2: the three flip statistics, M4 and M5 medians, bit-identical (target, seed) and samples (light-fetch files, mmCIF timestamp masked); RC(Gna) minus RC(G); NA(G) = G r1 against Gna r1 (as DET in the main study); HW(V6,Gna) beside HW(V6,G) and HW(V6,Gd) (those two as registered, copied, no difference computed), descriptive; cost, the ratio of the median process wall time and compile time, overall and per bucket, Gna to G, r1 processes, with the compiled device memory ratio. (e) Reading rules, fixed now. (1) "Recompilation bit-identical without autotuning" if every (target, seed) of Gna r2 is identical to Gna r1 in the compared files; otherwise the number that differ (a (target, seed) whose files are missing on a side is not identical). (2) The top-1 flip rate of recompilation is "reduced without autotuning" if the 95% interval of RC(Gna) minus RC(G) lies below 0, "not reduced" otherwise (the same rule for the success flip and flips that matter, as secondary). (3) If (2) fails, the paper states that autotuning alone does not account for them, whatever (1) shows; if both (1) and (2) hold, the paper states that turning autotuning off removed the recompilation differences on the L4, consistent with kernel selection by timing as their source; if (1) fails and (2) holds, that it reduced them. No direction is predicted. (f) Provisioning: L4 Spot, `MAX_L4=24` (all 24 parts at once, at the end of the launch plan), the launcher's L4 zones with us-east4-a and -c last; a part whose Spot VM is preempted or lost once (Spot preemption, VM gone, maintenance event) is relaunched at once on demand (`PROVISIONING=standard` on its line, written by the retry wrapper `prep_retry_arm.sh` and by the supervisor, both outside the repository), where the project has an on-demand quota for that accelerator (L4, A100, v6e; the project has no on-demand H100 quota, console of 2026-11-21, so an H100 part is relaunched on Spot again, as in follow-ups 9, 14 and 15); every later launch of that part is on demand; each fallback is listed in the completion row (the speed of the results comes before their cost: decision of 2026-11-21). A part that obtained no VM is retried unchanged; parts of whole targets (`make_prep_plans.py --label aut`: 24 parts); the light fetch; guarded launcher and supervisor as follow-up 17. The wrappers of follow-ups 17 to 21 run in the order and with the MAX values of the launch plan (`COSTI_E_CALENDARIO.md`, outside the repository): GPUS_ALL_REGIONS 32 (approved on 2026-11-21), at most 32 GPUs at once. (g) Cost: from G's medians per bucket (124.3, 219.8, 399.5 and 706.1 s) and overheads, times 1.0 and 1.5 (autotuning off may select slower kernels; no measurement exists on these targets): 600 processes, 56.48 VM h, 30.42 USD expected (43.72 worst without preemption) at 0.5383 USD/h; with the fallback of item f, the worst case of each part includes a fallback allowance: its planned worst case (the VM lost at the end of its worst job) plus a share of its worst case at the fallback's price (on demand; Spot again for the H100), the share of parts assumed to fall back (L4 0.75, A100 0.5, H100 0.25, v6e 1.0; the shares of parts that lost a Spot VM in the run records up to 2026-11-15 were about 0.5, 0.25 and 0.1, and the v6e lacked Flex-start capacity for hours in follow-up 13); the realistic estimate takes shares of 0.5, 0.25, 0.1 and 0.5, the VM lost halfway through the expected job and the part rerun at the fallback's expected cost (`scripts/monitor/prep_budget.py costs`): 48.19 realistic, 98.42 worst with fallback; cap 115 USD (the worst case with fallback times 1.15, rounded up to 5 USD); budget and stop rules as follow-up 17 (`credit_check_prep.sh aut l4 BALANCE`, `results/aut_cap_stop.txt`). Entry of 2026-10-09, before any official-weights run. Interpretations as implemented in `analysis/`:

1. PoseBusters protein (M1): the checks run on the superposed predicted ligand with the predicted protein, moved by the same pocket superposition, as the conditioning protein (`dock` configuration). The crystal protein is available as an option (`--pb_protein crystal`) and is not the default.
2. Structure (M6): one (target, seed) prediction with its 5 samples. Cost per structure = run_alphafold process wall time / seeds in the process × hourly price; cost per sample (divided by 5) is also reported. VM setup and idle time are not included.
3. Amortised compilation (M6): wall − compile + compile / batch, the batch being the number of same-bucket targets among the 60 (19, 19, 14 and 8 for buckets 256, 512, 768 and 1024), a parameter of `analysis/cost.py`. Warm reruns are reported as measured, not amortised again.
4. Memory (M7): the compiled peak plus arguments is reported as written. XLA's compiled peak already includes the arguments on GPU (peak = temporaries + arguments + output) but not on TPU, so on GPU the sum counts the arguments twice; the compiled peak alone is reported as well. The CUDA allocator preallocates its pool and reports no reservation (0), so on GPU the allocator's peak bytes in use is reported instead. CPU: max RSS.
5. HW − RC (section 5): P is the first platform of each pair as named in section 5 (V6 for V6 vs G, V5 for V5 vs V6, G for G vs C), on the same resampled targets. HW − RC(Q) is also reported, labelled secondary. RC(C) does not exist in the pilot (C runs r1 only).
6. SD (section 5): pairs of seeds on r1. Its top-1 flip compares sample indices of different seeds, which are unrelated draws; it is reported with that note, and only its success flip and M4/M5 are read as informative.
7. Pilot check 3 (section 6): top-1 success on V6 uses the overall top-1 across seeds (as AlphaFold3 ranks), r1. The top-1 success per seed is reported as secondary.
8. Bit-identical (M4, pilot check 2): in `analysis/contrasts.py`, the mmCIF coordinates are identical as written. The bit-for-bit comparison of all outputs is `harness/compare_runs.py`.
9. Q3: each condition's success rate is computed on its own targets, and each pairwise difference on that pair's common targets (C covers 6 of the 10 pilot targets).
10. M4 reference ("first prediction"): side A of the contrast, the first-named condition. Prediction B is superposed on A by the pocket C-alpha atoms (pocket from the crystal, as in M1), and the ligand RMSD is symmetry-corrected. The RMSD does not depend on which side is the reference.
11. Pilot provisioning for G: on-demand (not Spot, and not Flex-start, which has no published price for g2-standard-8), with the launcher's default L4 zone list.
12. Correction: the earlier v5e vs v6e figure of 0.91 to 1.19 Å (random weights, libtpu 0.0.43.2, 7U3J and 7D5C, 5 samples each) was all-atom RMSD without superposition (`harness/compare_samples.py`), not RMSD after global superposition. On the same samples it reproduces as 0.907 to 1.186 Å; M4 as defined here gives a protein C-alpha RMSD after global superposition of 0.88 to 1.11 Å and a ligand RMSD after pocket superposition of 0.60 to 1.42 Å.

Pilot schedule: the L4 arm (G) of the pilot will run later than the TPU (V5, V6) and CPU (C) arms, because a GPU quota increase is pending.

## 11. Numeric precision (amendment of 2026-10-10, before any analysis)

**What the audit found** (AF3 v3.0.4 source, jax 0.10.2, tokamax 0.0.12):
- AF3 uses the same dtypes on every platform, CPU included: bfloat16 trunk and confidence Pairformer, float32 diffusion module, per-atom conditioning and output heads. Nothing in AF3, the patch or the harness changes them.
- Float32 matmuls without an explicit precision (most of the diffusion module, the per-atom conditioning, single-attention logits, the distogram and confidence logits) run at the backend's default: single-pass bfloat16 operands on TPU, TensorFloat-32 on the L4, IEEE float32 on CPU.
- On the L4, Tokamax runs a Triton kernel for the gated linear units (heuristic configuration, no runtime autotuning); TPU and CPU fall back to XLA. This holds in every L4 arm and cannot be changed without modifying AF3 code.
- The grid attention in the `xla` arm uses the same explicit precision (bfloat16 operands, float32 accumulation) on every platform.

**Consequence for the contrasts of section 5:**
- V5 vs V6 isolates the chip within one compiler and precision policy.
- V6 vs G and G vs C measure the whole platform stack as AF3 runs by default, including the precision and GLU-kernel differences. The paper says so explicitly and does not attribute these contrasts to the chip alone.

**Precision-matched arms (added):**

| Code | Platform | Setting | Role |
|---|---|---|---|
| V6h | TPU v6e | `xla`, `JAX_DEFAULT_MATMUL_PRECISION=highest` | precision-matched arm |
| Gh | NVIDIA L4 | `xla`, `JAX_DEFAULT_MATMUL_PRECISION=highest` | precision-matched arm |

- `highest` makes float32 matmuls without explicit precision run as IEEE float32 on the L4 and as six-pass bfloat16 (close to float32) on TPU. It does not change bfloat16 matmuls, the explicit-precision sites or the attention. CPU already runs these matmuls in float32, so C serves as the reference for both arms and is not rerun.
- **Pilot:** V6h and Gh on the 10 pilot targets, seeds 1 and 2, r1 only, in their own sessions with the same frozen inputs and weights.
- **Main study:** V6h and Gh on all 60 targets, seeds 1 and 2 at least; whether to extend them is decided with the pilot (section 6) and logged in section 10.

**Added contrasts** (secondary, pre-specified, paired and computed as in section 5, each reported whatever it shows):

| Contrast | Pairs | Measures |
|---|---|---|
| **Precision** PR(P) | r1 on P vs r1 on Ph, P in {V6, G} | effect of the backend's default matmul precision on the same hardware |
| **Matched hardware** HWh(V6,G) | V6h vs Gh | platform difference with float32 matmuls matched |
| **Matched hardware** HWh(G,C) | Gh vs C | as above, against the float32 CPU reference |

**Reading rule, fixed now:** for each primary pair, the paper reports HW(P,Q) next to HWh(P,Q) and PR. A difference that shrinks to the RC level under matched precision is attributed to the default matmul precision; what remains is attributed to the platform (chip, compiler and, on the L4, the GLU kernel), without separating those further. No direction is predicted.

**Residual differences not controlled:** accumulation order and fusion choices of each compiler, six-pass bfloat16 against IEEE float32, bfloat16 elementwise handling, and the L4's Triton GLU kernel. They are listed in the threats to validity.
